## management-governance — module synthesis

_Source: `99_management-governance-synthesis.md`_

# Management-Governance Module — INDIAMART (Synthesis)

## Abstract

IndiaMART is run by its two founders, who together hold 49.08% of the company bought at incorporation in 1999, not granted through options, and have led it continuously for 27 years — real, not narrative, alignment. Capital allocation is mixed-to-strong: dividends are well covered, growth is self-funding with near-zero capex, and every deal was cash-funded with zero debt, but a ~₹31 billion treasury pile has grown with no buyback since September 2023 and the acquisition/venture program remains unproven on returns. The clearest weakness is incentive design: the annual bonus's "Balanced Scorecard" metrics are never disclosed, and CEO cash pay rose 11.17% in FY26 while diluted EPS fell 14.0% and the share price fell 9.75% — a direct, dated divergence. Verdict: standard/mixed stewardship — genuinely owner-led and candid, but not yet demonstrably paying for per-share results.

## 1. Stewardship Verdict

- **Verdict:** Standard / mixed
- **Hard disqualifier flagged (business-model/01)?** N — `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md` found 0 of 8 disqualifiers triggered and 0 of 5 near-misses in band. One soft integrity signal (a Designated Person share pledge made without pre-clearance, and a separate one-share closed-window trade) was routed to this module, investigated at the primary source (the audited FY26 Secretarial Audit Report) by `01_management-and-track-record.md`, and found disclosed, self-reported, and immaterial — **RF-MGT-005 is NOT emitted.**
- Management quality /100: **56**
- Capital allocation /100: **70**
- Incentive alignment /100: **32**
- Shareholder friendliness /100: **74** *(average of `04`'s Ownership Alignment Score 73/100 and `05`'s Board & Shareholder Rights Score 74/100 — see Section 3 for the reconciliation logic)*
- Disclosure candor /100: **79**
- Governance risk /100 *(higher = worse)*: **38**
- Data quality /100: **90** *(from `00`: pool rated "Sufficient," no active partial-data caps on any of the six specialists)*
- Overall usefulness /100: **90**
- Insider ownership (one line): Promoter & Promoter Group hold **49.17%** (audited, FY26 Corporate Governance Report, 18 holders), of which the two founders (Dinesh Chandra Agarwal 29.09%, Brijesh Kumar Agrawal 19.99%) hold **49.08%** directly — founding equity, not option grants; no promoter-level pledge is disclosed.
- Biggest governance signal (one line): CEO cash pay rose **+11.17%** in FY26 while diluted EPS fell **−14.0%** and the share price fell **−9.75%** in the same fiscal year, against an annual-bonus metric ("Balanced Scorecard") whose contents are never itemized anywhere in the FY22–FY26 disclosure record.
- **Governance Score /100** — `0.20×CapAlloc + 0.18×Incentive + 0.18×ShFriendliness + 0.16×Candor + 0.16×MgmtQuality + 0.12×(100 − GovRisk)`:
  `0.20×70 + 0.18×32 + 0.18×74 + 0.16×79 + 0.16×56 + 0.12×(100−38)`
  `= 14.00 + 5.76 + 13.32 + 12.64 + 8.96 + 7.44 = 62.12` → **62/100**
- **Confidence-Adjusted Governance Score /100** (= Governance Score × Confidence / 100): `62.12 × 88 / 100 = 54.67` → **55/100**
- **Governance Rating** (mapped from the raw Governance Score, per MODULE_RULES: 85–100 Excellent · 70–84 Good · 55–69 Watchlist · 40–54 Weak · <40 Avoid): **Watchlist** (62 falls in the 55–69 band)
- **Confidence Score /100** (source quality): **88** — the pool has a current audited proxy-equivalent (27th AGM Notice + Integrated Annual Report FY2025-26), a full board/committee export, 11 quarters of verbatim (not paraphrased) transcripts, and audited RPT/pledge/secretarial disclosure (mostly confidence 4–5 sources); docked for the compensation-metric opacity (the Balanced Scorecard itself is undisclosed, not a sourcing failure but a content gap), the absence of a dedicated per-transaction insider-trading (SAST/PIT) filing (confidence 1–2 on that narrow slice), and the lack of any governance peer set.
- **Red-Flag Count / Critical Red-Flag Count:** **5 / 0**

## 1A. Module Disconfirmation

- **Strongest bear point:** The incentive-pay structure is opaque on the one question that matters, and the one year checkable in the data shows it failed its own sanity check — CEO cash pay +11.17% while diluted EPS fell 14.0% and the share price fell 9.75% (FY26), with the underlying "Balanced Scorecard" metrics never itemized across FY22–FY26 [`03_incentives-and-compensation.md`, rows 03-004, 03-006, 03-007]. Layered on top: a 27-year, ~49%-owning founder bloc has zero disclosed succession plan [`01_management-and-track-record.md`, row 01-007], and the core growth KPI (paying-supplier net-adds) was guided at 8,000–9,000/quarter in April 2024 and abandoned within the same fiscal year, with no replacement figure given more than a year later [`01`, row 01-013; `06_candor-and-disclosure-quality.md`, Section 1].
- **Strongest bull point (steelman):** The founders' 49.08% stake was bought at incorporation in 1999, not granted — real capital at risk for 27 years, with the company debt-free throughout and every acquisition/buyback/dividend self-funded from operating cash flow [`02_capital-allocation-scorecard.md`, row 02-013]. Management is directly, unpromotionally candid in bad quarters: it led Q1 FY27 prepared remarks with a negative supplier number and two named causes before any question was asked, and on the Q4 FY25 call the CEO refused to fabricate a recovery number under direct pressure ("I'm really not confident to give you any answer as of now") [`06`, Section 4]. The board clears independence mechanics with room to spare (62.5% independent, no designated Chairman, fully independent audit/NRC committees) [`05_board-and-shareholder-rights.md`, Section 1].
- **Single killer risk:** Undisclosed pay metrics plus a founder-controlled board (no designated Chairman, CEO and co-founder hold two of eight board seats and ~49% of the vote) mean there is no external check forcing the Balanced Scorecard to reward per-share value if the founders choose otherwise — the one data point available (FY26) shows pay rising as per-share value fell, and minority shareholders have no visibility into whether that will recur.
- **Disconfirming evidence already visible:** A minority-shareholder check IS working in practice — the Amit Agarwal (promoter-family) related-party "office or place of profit" appointment drew 21.72% of votes against in the FY26 postal ballot [`05`, row 05-013, RF-RPT-002/RF-SHR-001], showing institutional and public shareholders (50.88% of the register) are alert and willing to vote against founder-adjacent items even though the resolution still passed.

## 3. Reconciliation

Three real tensions surfaced across the six specialists, reconciled here to the more conservative reading:

1. **Candor (79/100, strong) vs. incentive alignment (32/100, weak).** `06` finds management genuinely candid about operating misses — leading calls with bad numbers, giving itemized explanations, refusing to fabricate a recovery figure under pressure. `03` finds the compensation structure opaque and, on the one year checkable, misaligned with per-share results. These are not contradictory (verbal candor about *results* is a different thing from *disclosure of pay-metric design*), but they should not be allowed to average into a comfortable middle. **Reconciled view:** both scores stand at face value; the module does not let the strong candor score soften the weak incentive-alignment finding, and the Governance Score formula's 0.18 weight on incentive alignment (vs. 0.16 on candor) reflects that MODULE_RULES already treats the pay-alignment question as at least as decision-relevant as the tone-of-voice question.

2. **Capital allocation (70/100, strong-lower-end) vs. management quality (56/100, mixed).** `02` finds capital stewardship genuinely strong (self-funding growth, debt-free, covered dividends), while `01` finds a materially below-par record of hitting the specific numeric targets management sets for itself in public. **Reconciled view:** no contradiction — these are different domains (capital stewardship vs. operating-target delivery) and both scores are carried through unchanged; a reader should not infer that strong capital discipline offsets a weak promise-vs-delivery record, or vice versa.

3. **Ownership Alignment Score (73, from `04`) vs. Board & Shareholder Rights Score (74, from `05`).** These measure adjacent but distinct things (insider skin-in-the-game and pledge risk vs. board independence and RPT/voting mechanics) and do not conflict — both land in the same "strong, with real caveats" band. They are averaged into the single "Shareholder friendliness" score the Governance Score formula requires (73.5 → 74), rather than one being discarded, since MODULE_RULES defines a single Shareholder-friendliness axis and both specialist outputs are equally primary-sourced (confidence 4–5 throughout).

No other material disagreements: all specialists that touched the routed integrity signal (Designated Person pledge/closed-window trade) independently concur it is investigated, disclosed, self-reported, and immaterial — RF-MGT-005 is not emitted anywhere in the module.

## 5. Stewardship Summary

This team has, in large part, delivered per-share value: growth has been self-funding (near-zero capex, structurally negative operating working capital), every buyback and dividend was cash-funded with zero debt, and dividends have been covered in every year shown, including the tightest (2.3x FCF in FY26). But the record on the specific, dated, numeric targets management chooses to talk about publicly is weak — a missed FY24 margin target, a FY25 revenue-growth aspiration missed by roughly half even after being downgraded live on the same call, and a core supplier-growth KPI abandoned within the same fiscal year it was guided, with no replacement figure offered in the fifteen months since. Incentives point at size and tenure more than at per-share results as far as the record can be verified: the bonus's actual metrics are never disclosed, and the one year checkable shows CEO cash pay rising while diluted EPS and the share price both fell. Minority shareholders are not unprotected — the board clears independence mechanics comfortably, and a promoter-family RPT appointment drew a real 21.72% vote against, showing the mechanism works — but it took an active minority vote, not board self-policing, to register that objection, and the same board features a founder-controlled dynamic (no designated Chairman, ~49% of the vote concentrated in the two people running the company day to day, 27 years, zero disclosed succession plan) that leaves governance dependent on those two individuals' continued good judgment rather than on structural checks. The single most important reason to trust this team is that their own capital — bought at founding, not granted — has ridden alongside minority shareholders' for 27 years with no disclosed pledge and no evidence of opportunistic selling; the single most important reason for caution is that the compensation plan rewarding that same team is not demonstrably tied to the per-share outcomes those minority shareholders actually experience.

## 5D. Analyst Follow-Up Questions

- **RF-MGT-003 (KPI redefinition):** Is the ~9–10% FY26 growth band a durable new steady state, or will it be cut again if paying-supplier net-adds do not recover from the current near-zero/negative range? Is this company-specific execution or a sector-wide B2B-marketplace slowdown (cross-check against Just Dial's own paying-customer trend)?
- **RF-RPT-002 / RF-SHR-001 (Amit Agarwal appointment, 21.72% against):** What is Mr. Agarwal's actual role and title (not disclosed beyond "relative of KMP")? Is the position renewed in FY27, and does the ₹10.14mn FY26 remuneration escalate? Was the 21.72% dissent driven primarily by institutional holders (32.25% FPI+MF+AIF) or retail — and does the board plan any governance-process change (e.g., pre-vetting family appointments through the NRC before they reach a shareholder ballot) in response?
- **RF-DISC-002 ("onetime" investment gains):** Is the recurring investment-gains income stream (₹1,038mn–₹2,669mn/year, 5 straight years) material to a normalized EPS or valuation view? Will management ever characterize it as structurally recurring rather than purely episodic, and does this affect how PBT/EPS should be read against EBITDA?
- **Incentive opacity (03-004, undisclosed Balanced Scorecard):** What specific KPIs and weights sit inside the Balanced Scorecard — are any of them per-share or ROIC-based, or is it entirely revenue/EBITDA/scale-based (consistent with what management emphasizes publicly)? Is the company willing to disclose this to investors going forward?
- **CEO pay-performance divergence (03-006/007):** Does the FY27 PLVC payout fall if diluted EPS and the share price remain weak — the direct, live test of whether the undisclosed scorecard is genuinely performance-sensitive, or whether FY26's divergence recurs?
- **Succession risk (01-007):** Does the Nomination & Remuneration Committee have an internal (undisclosed) succession plan for either founder, and would the company be willing to disclose even a high-level continuity framework given the 27-year concentrated tenure and ~49% combined voting power?
- **Non-founder C-suite churn (01-006):** Were the CIO and CHRO departures (Apr 2024–Jan 2025) voluntary, performance-related, or restructuring-driven? Does further senior non-founder turnover recur in FY27/FY28?

## 7. Note To The Final Synthesizer

- **Stewardship verdict:** Standard / mixed. Strongest evidence for it: two founders hold 49.08% of the company, bought at founding in 1999, not granted, and both remain full-time executives 27 years later — genuine capital at risk alongside minority holders, with no promoter pledge disclosed and no evidence of opportunistic selling.
- **Capital-allocation record:** Broadly per-share value-creative but not clean — self-funding organic growth, zero debt, covered dividends throughout FY22–FY26 (Capital allocation 70/100), offset by a treasury pile that has grown to ~₹31bn with no buyback since September 2023, and an acquisition/venture program (Busy Infotech + ~19 minority checks) that remains unproven on returns three-plus years in.
- **Incentive/ownership alignment:** Ownership is real and aligned (49.08% founding equity); the compensation *plan* is not demonstrably aligned — the annual bonus's Balanced Scorecard metrics are never disclosed, and CEO cash pay rose 11.17% in FY26 while diluted EPS fell 14.0% and the share price fell 9.75% (Incentive alignment 32/100, the module's weakest score).
- **Biggest governance risk / red flag:** No single item is severe on its own (0 Critical-severity flags), but the combination of undisclosed pay metrics + a founder-controlled board (no designated Chairman, ~49% of the vote held by the two operating executives) + the one checkable year showing pay diverging from per-share results is the module's central caution. Secondary items: a promoter-family RPT appointment drew 21.72% shareholder opposition (RF-RPT-002/RF-SHR-001), and recurring "onetime" investment-gain labeling understates a genuinely persistent income category (RF-DISC-002).
- **Hard disqualifier flagged by business-model/01_disqualifier-scan:** None. Verbatim: *"Any disqualifier triggered: N... No verdict lock applies from this scan. The synthesizer is free to score the business on its merits."* A soft integrity signal (Designated Person pledge/closed-window trade) was routed, investigated at the primary source, and cleared — RF-MGT-005 not emitted.
- **§24 rejector filters tripped:** None applied a hard cap. Filter 2 (turnaround) — not applicable, no turnaround thesis. Filter 4 (serial acquirer, RF-CAP-004) — flagged for monitoring at severity 58/100, below the ≥70 hard-cap threshold; charged to the capital-allocation score's M&A-discipline sub-component instead of a separate hard cap. Filter 6 (unaligned owner, RF-OWN-004) — not triggered; standalone founder-operated company. Filter 1 (integrity, RF-MGT-005) — investigated and cleared, not emitted.
- **Partial-data caps:** None applied. `00_governance-data-triage.md` rated the pool "Sufficient" with no active caps on any of the six specialists; the only residual gap is a narrower one (no dedicated SAST/PIT per-transaction insider-trading filing), which lowered confidence on specific ownership-flow rows (`04`) rather than triggering a blanket cap.
- **Biggest missing data point (single highest-value next request):** The NRC's Balanced Scorecard KPI list and weights for the annual executive bonus — without it, the module cannot confirm whether the FY26 pay-for-performance divergence (CEO pay +11.17% vs. EPS -14.0% and share price -9.75%) is a one-off anomaly or a structural feature of an undisclosed, scale-weighted pay design.
- **Explicit handoff:** This module supersedes the `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance` quick-read. The master synthesizer should treat this module's governance verdict (Standard / mixed), its Governance Score (62/100, Confidence-Adjusted 55/100), and its Governance Rating (Watchlist) as the primary governance read for INDIAMART.

## 8. Simple Summary

- Management has kept some promises and badly missed others: margin targets were eventually beaten (a year late, via cost cuts), but the core growth-driver metric (paying-supplier net-adds) was guided, missed by roughly 75%, and then quietly dropped with no replacement number given for over a year.
- Capital has mostly created per-share value — self-funding growth, zero debt, well-covered dividends — but a growing ₹31 billion cash-and-investments pile with no buyback in almost two years, plus an unproven acquisition/venture-investing program, are real drags on that record.
- The pay plan actually rewards something undisclosed: the annual bonus's metrics are never itemized in any year reviewed, and in the one year checkable, the CEO's cash pay went up while earnings per share and the stock price both went down.
- Skin in the game is real and large — the two founders own 49% of the company, bought at founding, not granted — but no promoter-level pledge disclosure exists to independently confirm a clean pledge position, and neither founder has an ESOP stake (they're contractually excluded).
- Minority shareholders are structurally protected (independent board, no dual-class stock, active voting rights) but had to actively use those rights — a family appointment for a promoter relative only got a real check because over a fifth of votes cast went against it.
- Management is candid when results are bad: it leads earnings calls with the negative numbers before being asked, and on the worst quarter in the record the CEO flatly admitted he couldn't forecast a recovery rather than making something up.
- No hard governance disqualifier was flagged (no audit qualification, no going-concern issue, no promoter pledge above threshold, no related-party leakage above threshold, no restatement, no regulatory enforcement).
- This module is highly useful for the master synthesizer: the data pool was rated "Sufficient" with zero missing-data caps across all six specialists, so the scores below are built on primary sources (audited filings, verbatim transcripts), not inference.

```red_flags.csv
red_flag_id,trigger,severity,evidence,source_and_date,score_impact,follow_up
RF-MGT-003,"Management changes a KPI after underperformance",High,"Growth aspiration walked from 33% to 20-25% to <10% over 4 quarters; supplier net-add guidance (8000-9000/qtr) abandoned same fiscal year set","01_management-and-track-record.md rows 01-010/01-012/01-013; Q4 FY24 transcript 2024-04-30; Q4 FY25 transcript 2025-04-29","Management quality capped at 56/100 (mixed band); contributes to Governance risk 38/100","Confirm whether ~9-10% growth band is durable or cut again"
RF-RPT-002,"Promoter-linked related-party transaction",High,"Amit Agarwal (promoter-family relative) office-or-place-of-profit appointment; FY26 remuneration INR 10.14mn; RPT vote drew 21.72% against","05_board-and-shareholder-rights.md row 05-013; FY26 Annual Report Postal Ballot results, 2025-08-29","Shareholder friendliness held at 74/100 rather than higher; contributes to Governance risk","Monitor role/renewal in FY27"
RF-SHR-001,"High votes against a key resolution",High,"Amit Agarwal RPT postal-ballot resolution drew 21.72% against (7374238 of 33955911 shares polled), vs 0.0032% against on the companion ID-appointment resolution","05_board-and-shareholder-rights.md row 05-020; FY26 Annual Report Postal Ballot results table","AGM-opposition/minority-protection sub-score held to 5/10 by 05; Shareholder friendliness capped below strong","Monitor FY27 votes-against on RPT/remuneration items"
RF-DISC-002,"Recurring one-off / aggressive non-GAAP add-backs",High,"Investment-revaluation gains labeled onetime (ProcMart Q3 FY23/Q4 FY24, Baldor Q3 FY26) recurred at material size every year FY22-FY26 (INR 1038mn-2669mn/yr)","06_candor-and-disclosure-quality.md row 06-007; earnings/06_earnings-quality.md §5/§7","Disclosure candor held to 79/100 rather than very strong; contributes to Governance risk","Monitor whether management flags this as structural"
Unregistered-pay-performance-divergence,"CEO cash pay rose while both available per-share metrics fell same fiscal year",High,"CEO total cash pay +11.17% YoY FY26; diluted EPS -14.0% (91.59 to 78.77); share price -9.75% (2331.00 to 2103.60)","03_incentives-and-compensation.md rows 03-006/03-007; FY26 Annual Report Section 197(12) table","Incentive alignment capped at 32/100 (weak band) - single largest driver of the module's low incentive score","Track whether FY27 PLVC falls if EPS/price stay weak"
```

## 6. Sufficiency Verdict

- **Verdict:** Sufficient
- **Reason:** The pool has a current India proxy-equivalent (27th AGM Notice + Integrated Annual Report FY2025-26, filed 2026-06-02, with Corporate Governance Report, BRSR, and postal-ballot voting results), a full board/committee export, a multi-year compensation export cross-checked against the CG Report's current-year remuneration table, a beneficial-ownership table (CG Report + CIQ), related-party and secretarial-audit disclosure, and a multi-year (FY2016–LTM Jun-2026) capital-allocation history — so all six specialists can run without a cap from missing data.
- **Specialists that can run:** management track record, capital allocation, incentives, ownership, board, candor — all six.
- **Hard disqualifier already flagged by business-model/01_disqualifier-scan?** N — 0 of 8 disqualifiers triggered, 0 of 5 near-misses in band [`business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md`]. One soft integrity signal is noted there (a Secretarial Audit finding of a Designated-Person pledge made without pre-clearance, and a one-share closed-window trade by another Designated Person) — both are disclosed, self-reported, and immaterial in magnitude, routed to this module as a minor compliance-control flag rather than a disqualifier.
- **Active partial-data caps:** None from the module's Score Cap Rules table. One narrower content gap to flag for agent `04`: the pool has no dedicated per-transaction insider buy/sell (SAST/PIT) filing — only the two Secretarial Audit compliance-lapse items — so agent `04`'s "recent net insider buying/selling (12 months)" read will be limited to what the ownership-activity table and Key Developments feed show, not a full insider-transaction ledger.
- **Critical missing items:** None.
- **Single highest-value missing document:** A dedicated SAST/PIT (Prohibition of Insider Trading) transaction-disclosure filing or exchange intimation log, to give agent `04` a real per-transaction insider buy/sell history rather than relying on the Secretarial Audit's two compliance-lapse mentions and the CIQ institutional-ownership flow table.

## management-governance / 01_management-and-track-record.md

# Management & Track Record — INDIAMART

Cross-module inputs used: `business-model/02_business-identity.md` (control context), `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md` (integrity soft-signal routed here), `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` and `business-model/12_red-flags-sweep.md` (management-turnover and key-person facts), `earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md` (current guidance/consensus behaviour), `earnings/01_historical-financials.md` (audited margin/growth trend used to check promise vs delivery). No `ciq_facts.json` sidecar exists for this run (confirmed empty at `analyses/INDIAMART_2026-08-13/_pool_extracts/ciq_facts.json`); all figures below are read directly from the CIQ workbook extracts, the FY26 Integrated Annual Report, and the verbatim earnings-call transcripts in the data pool.

## 1. Key Executives

| Role | Name | Appointed | Tenure | Relevant Background | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| CEO / MD | Dinesh Chandra Agarwal | Founded the company 1999; CEO, MD & Executive Director since September 13, 1999 | ~27 years | Founder; prior roles at HCL Technologies (USA), HCL Ltd, HCL Hewlett-Packard, C-DOT, CMC Ltd before founding IndiaMART's predecessor (InterMESH Systems); B.Tech (Computer Science), Harcourt Butler Technological Institute | [CIQ Professionals export; FY26 Annual Report, Board's Report] |
| Co-Founder / Whole-Time Director | Brijesh Kumar Agrawal | Co-founded 1999; Whole-Time Director since September 13, 1999 | ~27 years | Co-founder; former COO of IndiaMART; prior supply-chain consulting at Miebach Logistics GmbH; also Independent Director of Shiprocket Limited since March 2025 | [CIQ Professionals export] |
| CFO | Jitin Diwan | June 15, 2024 | ~2 years | Chartered Accountant (ICAI); prior Head of Finance (VP) at Upstox Securities; earlier roles at Amazon India, Bharti Airtel, Vodafone | [CIQ Professionals export; FY26 Annual Report, Note 33 (KMP), "Chief Financial Officer (with effect from 15 June 2024)"] |
| Prior CFO | Prateek Chandra | April 30, 2015 – June 14, 2024; now Chief Strategy Officer since June 15, 2024 | ~9 years as CFO | Chartered Accountant; joined IndiaMART Feb 2015; prior roles at KPMG, EXL, Hindustan Times Media | [CIQ Professionals export; FY26 Annual Report, Note 33 (KMP)] |
| COO | Dinesh Gulati | Joined March 12, 2012; COO title held since | ~14 years at the company | Prior Group President, Indian Express; prior roles at Bharti Airtel, Reliance Communications, Eastman Kodak | [CIQ Professionals export] |
| Group General Counsel, Company Secretary & Whole-Time Director | Manoj Bhargava | With the company since December 28, 2017; Whole-Time Director and Company Secretary since 2025; was Compliance Officer until January 21, 2025 | ~9 years at company | Qualified Company Secretary (ICSI); law and secretarial background at HT Media, Varun Beverages and others | [CIQ Professionals export; Board Members export] |

**Founder / owner-operator status:** the CEO and MD (Dinesh Chandra Agarwal) and the Whole-Time Director (Brijesh Kumar Agrawal) are the company's two co-founders, have held their executive roles continuously since incorporation in 1999 (27 years, unbroken), and together hold roughly 49% of the company directly (Agarwal 29.09%, Agrawal 19.99%) [FY26 Annual Report, p.7219, Promoter & Promoter Group Shareholding; CIQ Public Ownership Summary export, data as of Mar-31-2026]. This is a genuine founder-led, owner-operator company by CLAUDE.md §21's own test: skin in the game bought at founding, not merely granted.

## 2. Management Stability

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Senior departures (last 3 years) | CFO Prateek Chandra moved from CFO to Chief Strategy Officer (June 2024) — retained, not a departure. CIO Vivek Agrawal departed sometime around FY24 (last recorded compensation FY24 ₹10.51mn; no FY25 entry; replaced by Nikhil S. Prabhakar effective April 5, 2024 — no reason for the change disclosed). Compliance Officer Manoj Bhargava was promoted to Whole-Time Director (2025) — retained, not a departure — succeeded as Compliance Officer by Vasudha Bagri (January 22, 2025). Chief HR Officer Saurabh Deep Singla appointed November 2024 (predecessor and reason for change not disclosed in the pool). | [CIQ Professionals export; CIQ Compensation Summary export — "Agrawal, Vivek, Former Chief Information Officer," compensation stops after FY2024; FY26 Annual Report, Note 33 (KMP)] |
| CFO turnover | One CFO change in the roughly 9 years covered by the transcript archive (2015–2024): the prior CFO of 9 years (Prateek Chandra) was moved internally to Chief Strategy Officer rather than exiting the company, and the incoming CFO (Jitin Diwan) is externally hired with prior public-company finance experience (Upstox, Amazon India, Bharti Airtel). This is a single, internally-managed transition — not the "sudden CFO resignation" pattern the Red-Flag Trigger Engine (RF-MGT-001) targets. | [CIQ Professionals export; FY26 Annual Report, Note 33 (KMP)] |
| CEO succession / continuity | No succession plan, deputy-CEO designate, or emergency-continuity disclosure was found anywhere in the reviewed pool (Board's Report, Corporate Governance Report, or director bios), despite both founders having held their executive roles for the full 27-year life of the company and together controlling ~49% of the vote. `business-model/12_red-flags-sweep.md` independently flags this as "founder key-person concentration with no disclosed succession plan" (severity 35/100, inverted scale). | [FY26 Annual Report, Board's Report / Corporate Governance Report / Nomination & Remuneration Committee terms of reference — no succession disclosure found; `business-model/12_red-flags-sweep.md`, Section 2] |

**Read on stability:** the top of the house (both founders, 27 years) is exceptionally stable, and the one CFO transition in nine years was handled by retaining the outgoing CFO in a senior role rather than losing him — a genuine positive signal, not a warning. Below that, three non-founder C-suite seats (CIO, Compliance Officer, CHRO) turned over within an 18-month window (April 2024–January 2025) without disclosed reasons; none of the replacements carry any adverse signal on their own, but the concentration of changes in a short window, combined with zero disclosed succession planning for either founder, is a watch item — not a red flag on its own, but a gap that should be closed before the founders' continuous 27-year tenure becomes a genuine single point of failure.

## 3. Stated Strategy & Targets

IndiaMART's stated strategy is to grow the core marketplace by adding paying suppliers and moving existing suppliers up the Silver→Gold→Platinum tiers (category-based differential pricing), while scaling the Busy Infotech accounting-software subsidiary and deploying free cash flow into a portfolio of minority stakes in adjacent MSME-tech startups [`business-model/02_business-identity.md`, Section 2; `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md`, Section 1]. **The company issues no formal, numeric, company-wide guidance for consolidated revenue, EBITDA, or EPS** — there is no investor-guidance deck in the pool, and quarterly board-outcome press releases carry no forward figures [`earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md`, Section 2]. The only checkable, dated targets in the data pool are qualitative statements made live on earnings calls: management's Q1 FY27 call target for Busy Infotech's revenue CAGR ("closer to anything between… 27% to 30%" over the next couple of years, with a longer 35–40% aspiration explicitly framed as "work in progress") and a target of resolving core-platform silver-tier supplier churn within "2, 3 quarters" [`earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md`, Section 2, citing Q1 FY27 transcript, Jul 21, 2026]. Because there is no formal numeric guidance today, this section's promise-vs-delivery test below relies on the specific, dated, numeric targets management stated live on prior-year calls — which is the correct application of the partial-data rule, since those targets exist and are checkable even though the company does not publish them as formal guidance documents.

## 4. Promise vs Delivery

| Prior Promise / Target | Period Set | Actual Outcome | Kept / Missed | Source |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Consolidated EBITDA margin: "28% to 29% EBITDA margins in FY '24," with a target to "reach 30% by the end of this year [FY24]" | Set on Q4 FY23 call, Apr 28, 2023, for FY24 | FY24 consolidated EBITDA margin was 26.54% (down 2 bps from FY23's 26.57%) | **Missed** — came in ~2–3 points below the guided range and well short of the 30% target | `Q4 FY23 transcript, Q&A (Dinesh Agarwal)`; [`earnings/01_historical-financials.md`, EBITDA Margin % row and Section 6 narrative, citing CIQ Financials export] |
| Consolidated EBITDA margin: "a lot more confident of 30%-plus margin" (for FY25) | Set on Q4 FY24 call, Apr 30, 2024, for FY25 | FY25 consolidated EBITDA margin jumped to 37.03% (+1,048 bps YoY) | **Kept, and exceeded** — but delivered through cost discipline / sales-and-marketing-cost reduction on a slower revenue base, not the growth-driven operating leverage management had been describing when it set the target a year earlier | `Q4 FY24 transcript, Q&A (Dinesh Agarwal)`; [`earnings/01_historical-financials.md`, EBITDA Margin % row, citing CIQ Financials export] |
| Consolidated revenue / "collection" growth aspiration: "20% to 25% in the collection growth" standalone, "aspiration for 33% of the collection growth or revenue growth" consolidated | Set on Q4 FY24 call, Apr 30, 2024, for FY25 | FY25 consolidated revenue growth was 16.01% — roughly half the 33% consolidated aspiration and below the low end of the 20–25% standalone range | **Missed, materially** — CEO had already conceded on the same call that "30% at this juncture on a stand-alone basis sounds a little difficult," so this was a downgraded aspiration at the time it was set, and the company still missed it | `Q4 FY24 transcript, Q&A (Dinesh Agarwal)`; [`earnings/01_historical-financials.md`, Section 6 narrative — "annual growth ran… 16.01% (FY25)," citing CIQ Financials export] |
| Revenue / collection growth, walked down to "less than 10%… mid-single to high single digit… target around 10%" | Set on Q4 FY25 call, Apr 29, 2025, for FY26 | FY26 standalone Revenue from Operations grew 9% [`business-model/02_business-identity.md`]; FY26 consolidated revenue growth was 13.02% [`earnings/01_historical-financials.md`] | **Kept** at the standalone level (once the target itself had been cut roughly two-thirds from the original 20–33% aspiration); consolidated came in modestly above the cut target | `Q4 FY25 transcript, Q&A (Dinesh Agarwal)`; [`business-model/02_business-identity.md`, Section 2; `earnings/01_historical-financials.md`, Section 6] |
| Paying-supplier net additions: "8,000 to 9,000 suppliers every quarter… I would still try and see that anything upwards of 8,000 should be maintainable at least for this year" | Set on Q4 FY24 call, Apr 30, 2024, for FY25 | By Q4 FY25 (the same fiscal year the target covered), quarterly net supplier additions had fallen to 1,800–2,700, and management stated on that same call: "I'm really not confident to give you any answer as of now… I'll take another few quarters when I can give you answer on that side" | **Missed badly, and formal net-add guidance was withdrawn mid-year rather than restated** | `Q4 FY24 transcript, Q&A (Dinesh Agarwal / Swapnil Potdukhe)`; `Q4 FY25 transcript, Q&A (Dinesh Agarwal / Nikhil Choudhary)` |

**Pattern:** on the single metric management is most protective of in public commentary — the paying-supplier net-add run rate that drives the core marketplace's growth — the promise (8,000–9,000/quarter, stated in April 2024) was not walked down gradually; it was abandoned within the same fiscal year it was set, with the CEO declining to give any forward number. On revenue/collection growth, the pattern is a repeated downward revision of the target itself (33% aspiration in Apr-2024 → ~10% target in Apr-2025) rather than a single clean miss — this is a **KPI redefinition following underperformance**, which triggers **RF-MGT-003** (see Section 4A). On margin, the record is genuinely mixed: FY24 missed the guided range, but FY25 overshot the original 30%+ target by a wide margin — delivered later than promised, and through a different lever (cost control) than the one management had originally described.

**RF-MGT-003 (management changes a KPI after underperformance).** Triggered. Evidence: consolidated revenue/collection growth aspiration moved from "33%" (Q4 FY24 call, Apr 2024) to "20% to 25%" standalone (same call, already downgraded live) to "less than 10%… target around 10%" (Q4 FY25 call, Apr 2025) — a roughly two-thirds cut to the target over four quarters, tracking each quarter's actual underperformance rather than being set once and missed once. Severity: Medium (this is a live-call aspiration repeatedly walked down in real time, not a formal published target quietly buried) — but it is a genuine, dated, quantified pattern, not an impression. Follow-up: does the ~9–10% band represent a durable new steady state, or will it be cut again if net supplier adds do not recover from the current near-zero/negative range (Section 4A)?

## 4A. Turnaround & Integrity Tests (CLAUDE.md §24, Filters 1 & 2)

| Test | Finding | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Is the thesis a turnaround (struggling business + new management / new plan)? | **No.** IndiaMART is a profitable, debt-free, founder-led company with 27 years of continuous management; it is decelerating (revenue growth 30.78%→21.45%→16.01%→13.02% FY23–FY26) and facing an admitted buyer-side funnel slowdown, but this is a maturing-growth and execution-quality question, not a distressed-business-plus-new-management turnaround setup. | [`earnings/01_historical-financials.md`, Section 6; `business-model/12_red-flags-sweep.md`, Section 2] |
| If yes: years of *proven* operating inflection actually delivered (not promised) | Not applicable — no turnaround thesis to test. | — |
| Résumé / plan / consultant deck cited as the reason to believe? (not evidence) | Not applicable. The new CFO (Jitin Diwan, since June 2024) carries a strong résumé (Upstox, Amazon India, Bharti Airtel, Vodafone), but no part of this report treats that résumé as evidence of a turnaround or of the promise-vs-delivery outcomes above — those are judged on the numbers in Section 4, not on his background. | [CIQ Professionals export] |
| Routed integrity "buzz" from `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan` (unverified adverse signal) | **Investigated and found to be a disclosed, audited compliance-control lapse — not a fraud allegation and not unresolved "buzz."** The FY26 Secretarial Audit Report (an audited annual compliance document, reviewed as part of the FY26 Integrated Annual Report) states: "a Designated Person, created pledge on equity shares of the Company without seeking pre-clearance from the Compliance Officer," and separately "another Designated Person executed trade of one equity share of the Company during the closure of [the trading window]" — both self-reported by the company to the stock exchanges [FY26 Annual Report, Secretarial Audit Report, Annexure-A, p.7628–7645 range]. This report independently checked the primary source and confirmed the disqualifier-scan's own reading: no name, pledge size, or seniority level is disclosed for either Designated Person — that omission is standard practice for this class of routine secretarial-audit finding under Indian disclosure norms, not opacity (CLAUDE.md §27). Materiality: Low — a one-share trade is immaterial by definition, and the pledge finding is a pre-clearance *process* failure (the pledge itself, once made, was disclosed to the exchanges), not an allegation that shares were hidden or that a promoter breached a pledge ceiling; no promoter-level pledge percentage is disclosed anywhere in the pool, consistent with the disqualifier-scan's finding of "not disclosed / not computable — treated as 0%." **This is investigated and cleared** under the MODULE_RULES standard (primary evidence — the audited Secretarial Audit Report — describes the event, its self-reporting, and its immateriality) — it does not meet the bar for an unresolved adverse integrity signal, and **RF-MGT-005 is NOT emitted.** It is recorded here as a Low-materiality governance-control finding that lowers, marginally, the "transparency around misses" component of Section 5's score, not the integrity read. | [FY26 Annual Report, Secretarial Audit Report, Annexure-A; `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md`, Section 3 "Integrity note (soft signal, not a lock)"] |

**Turnaround rule:** not applicable — no conviction cap from Filter 2.

**Integrity rule:** applied — the routed signal was chased to the primary source (the audited Secretarial Audit Report itself, not a secondary summary) and found disclosed, self-reported, and immaterial. No RF-MGT-005; no conviction cap from Filter 1.

## 5. Management Read

The top of this company is as stable as management gets: two co-founders, 27 years in their executive seats, ~49% of the company owned directly since inception — real skin in the game, bought at founding rather than granted, and the one CFO transition in nine years was handled by retaining the outgoing CFO rather than losing him. But the promise-vs-delivery record on the metrics management chooses to talk about publicly is weak: the FY24 margin target was missed, the FY25 revenue-growth aspiration was missed by roughly half even after being downgraded live on the same call it was set, and the core paying-supplier net-add guidance (8,000–9,000/quarter) was abandoned within the same fiscal year it was given, with the CEO declining to offer a replacement number — a pattern that meets the bar for RF-MGT-003 (KPI changed after underperformance), not a one-off miss. Margin performance did eventually catch up and overshoot the original target, which is real credit, but it came a year late and via cost cuts rather than the growth story that was told when the target was set. The single biggest management signal is the combination of an unbroken 27-year founder tenure with zero disclosed succession plan, sitting directly on top of a core marketplace whose own management now openly admits the buyer-side funnel is stagnating — a genuine key-person and execution-quality risk that the record does not yet show this management team has a proven answer for.

## Management Quality Score

| Component | Score | Max Score | Evidence |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Stability and tenure | 14 | 20 | 27-year founder tenure at CEO and Whole-Time Director, clean single CFO succession (rows 01-001–01-004) offset by 3 non-founder C-suite changes in an 18-month window with undisclosed reasons and zero disclosed founder-succession plan (rows 01-006, 01-007) |
| Promise-vs-delivery | 12 | 30 | Two clear, material misses on dated, numeric targets (FY24 margin, FY25 revenue growth, and the abandoned supplier net-add guidance — rows 01-008, 01-010, 01-013) against one delayed-but-real margin overshoot (row 01-009) and one target hit only after being cut two-thirds (row 01-011); the pattern includes a KPI redefined after underperformance (RF-MGT-003) |
| Execution record | 15 | 25 | Revenue growth has decelerated every year for four straight years (30.78%→21.45%→16.01%→13.02%, FY23-FY26 [`earnings/01_historical-financials.md`]), margin has been volatile rather than trending, and management itself now admits buyer-side funnel stagnation (row 01-014) — the business remains profitable and cash-generative throughout, which caps the downside of this score |
| Succession / depth | 7 | 15 | No disclosed plan for either founder despite 27 years of unbroken, concentrated tenure and ~49% combined ownership (row 01-007); the one CFO succession that did occur was handled cleanly (row 01-004), which is the only positive data point on succession execution |
| Transparency around misses | 8 | 10 | Management repeatedly and candidly conceded misses live on calls (conceding the 30% margin target was "a little difficult," admitting the net-add guidance could not be maintained, directly answering the buyer-decline question on the Q1 FY27 call) rather than deflecting or obscuring — genuine candor, even though the underlying numbers were often bad news |
| Total | 56 | 100 | Mixed band (41-60): stable, well-owned founder leadership with a materially below-par record of delivering on the numeric targets it sets for itself in public, partially offset by real candor about the misses |

No partial-data cap applies to this score: the data pool contains eleven quarters of transcripts spanning FY22-FY27, audited annual reports for FY24-FY26, and the FY26 Secretarial Audit Report — sufficient to build both the executive profile and a genuine promise-vs-delivery record, so this is not a "no prior promises to check" case.

## management-governance / 02_capital-allocation-scorecard.md

_Source: `02_capital-allocation-scorecard.md`_

# Capital-Allocation Scorecard — INDIAMART

Cross-module inputs used: `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` (the quick-read this module deepens and supersedes for the capital-allocation read per MODULE_RULES), `management-governance/01_management-and-track-record.md` (promise-vs-delivery record, used to cross-check management's own framing of capital decisions), `earnings/01_historical-financials.md` (CFO, FCF, capex, EBITDA/EBIT, net-debt figures). No `ciq_facts.json` sidecar exists for this run (confirmed absent at `analyses/INDIAMART_2026-08-13/_pool_extracts/`); all figures below are this agent's own sourced read of the Capital IQ (CIQ) financial-statement exports, the FY25 Annual Report's notes to accounts, and verbatim earnings-call transcripts, cross-checked against each other.

**Jurisdiction / regime:** India, NSE:INDIAMART / BSE:542726. Reporting standard: Ind AS. Reporting currency: INR. Fiscal year ends 31 March. All figures in **₹ million** unless stated otherwise, consolidated basis. Period covered: **FY22–FY26** (years ended 31-Mar-2022 through 31-Mar-2026), the full 5-year window available in the pool — no partial-data cap applies to the scorecard's period coverage.

**A share-count caveat that governs every per-share and per-price figure below:** IndiaMART issued a **1:1 bonus share issue** on 22 June 2023 (during FY24), doubling shares outstanding from ~30.6 million to ~61.2 million [FY25 Annual Report, Note 2 to Equity, "Bonus issue during the year… 3,06,14,574"]. The Capital IQ balance-sheet share-count series is bonus-adjusted retroactively (shows ~61.1mn even for FY22), but the **nominal buyback prices disclosed in filings and on earnings calls are NOT bonus-adjusted** — they are the actual rupee amount paid per share at the time. Every buyback price below is shown both as the **nominal (as-disclosed)** figure and, where the deal predates the bonus, its **bonus-adjusted equivalent** (nominal ÷ 2), labelled explicitly.

---

## 1. Uses of Capital (FY22–FY26, 5 years)

Reporting currency: **INR million**. Denominator: cumulative Cash from Operations (CFO), FY22–FY26 = **₹27,547.40mn** [Capital IQ export, `Financials.xls`, Cash Flow tab, FY2022–FY2026 annual columns; cross-checked to `earnings/01_historical-financials.md`, Section 1].

| Use | Cumulative Amount | Currency | % of CFO | Source |
|---|---:|---|---:|---|
| Acquisitions (net) — control deal (Busy Infotech) + net growth in the minority-stake venture portfolio | 9,188.68 | INR mn | 33.4% | [Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab, "Cash Acquisitions" FY23 = −5,068.4 (Busy Infotech); Balance Sheet tab, "Long-term Investments" FY22 4,209.32 → FY26 8,329.6, net growth +4,120.28] |
| Buybacks (2 tender-offer buybacks, FY23 + FY24) | 7,394.5 | INR mn | 26.8% | [Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab, "Repurchase of Common Stock" FY23 −1,232.6, FY24 −6,161.9] |
| Dividends (cash paid) | 5,326.9 | INR mn | 19.3% | [Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab, "Common Dividends Paid" FY22–FY26] |
| Capex | 511.6 | INR mn | 1.9% | [Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab, "Capital Expenditure" FY22–FY26] |
| Debt/lease repayment (the company has no bank borrowings — this is entirely Ind AS 116 lease-liability amortization) | 619.3 | INR mn | 2.2% | [Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab, "Total Debt Repaid" FY22–FY26] |
| *Memo: growth in the treasury book (Trading Asset Securities — mutual funds/bonds; idle capital, not a deployment)* | *7,286.4* | *INR mn* | *26.5%* | *[Balance Sheet tab, "Trading Asset Securities" FY22 23,007.65 → FY26 30,294.05]* |

The five deployment lines (acquisitions, buybacks, dividends, capex, debt/lease repayment) sum to ₹23,040.98mn, **83.6% of cumulative CFO**. The remainder of CFO, plus proceeds from net treasury-securities sales in FY23/FY24, funded the ₹7,286.4mn growth in the parked treasury book — capital that generated returns as portfolio income (shown as "Gain (Loss) On Sale Of Invest.," ₹1,038mn–₹2,669mn/year, per `earnings/01_historical-financials.md` Section 3) but was not actively deployed into the operating business or returned to shareholders. **This bundling is a real data limitation, not a judgment call dressed as one:** Capital IQ's cash-flow statement nets minority-stake private-placement outflows against treasury mutual-fund/bond purchases and sales in a single "Invest. in Marketable & Equity Securt." line (FY22 −3,770.4, FY23 +1,056.24, FY24 +1,443.2, FY25 −5,455.9, FY26 −3,343.5), so the balance-sheet "Long-term Investments" growth (+4,120.28mn) is used above as the cleaner proxy for the venture-portfolio deployment, separate from the "Trading Asset Securities" treasury book — both are Capital IQ balance-sheet line items, not a company-disclosed sub-total, so this split is **inference, not from filings**.

## 2. M&A Scorecard

| Deal | Year | Price | Disclosed Rationale / Synergy | Delivered? | Opportunity Cost | Source |
|---|---|---:|---|---|---|---|
| Busy Infotech Pvt. Ltd. (accounting-software / SaaS for MSMEs) — control acquisition | Announced 2022-01-24, closed 2022-04-06 (FY23) | $66.93mm (≈₹5,068.4mn), all-cash, no debt drawn | Enter adjacent MSME accounting-software market, cross-sell to IndiaMART's existing paying-supplier base | **Partial / unproven on returns.** Segment revenue (Accounting Software Services) grew FY23 ₹433.73mn → FY24 ₹537.94mn (+24.0%) → FY25 ₹681.91mn (+26.8%) → FY26 ₹1,261.04mn (+84.9%, boosted by the Livekeeping bolt-on) — a real growth story. But segment **EBITDA has been negative for three straight years**: FY23 +₹101.75mn (profitable in the acquisition year) → FY24 −₹38.9mn → FY25 −₹119.2mn → FY26 −₹28.2mn (loss narrowing but still negative). Management's own FY27 target for Busy is "27% to 30%" revenue CAGR with a 35–40% aspiration explicitly framed as "work in progress" — i.e., even four years in, management is not yet claiming the segment has proven its economics. | Real: $66.93mm (~4.7% of the company's own ~₹107,319.4mn market cap as of 11-Aug-2026) diverted from buybacks/dividends into a segment still losing money at the operating line three years post-close; goodwill of ₹4,122.34mn sits on the balance sheet, subject to ongoing impairment testing (a Key Audit Matter, not yet impaired) | [Transaction Summary M&A/Private Placements, CIQ export]; [Financials.xls, Segments tab, FY2023–FY2026]; [FY26 Annual Report, Note 33 — Busy Infotech goodwill ₹4,122.34mn]; [Q1 FY27 transcript, Jul 21, 2026 — Busy CAGR target]; [Public Ownership Summary — market cap ₹107,319.4mn, Aug-11-2026] |
| ~19 minority private-placement / venture-style checks (Fleetx, Mobisy, Legistify, Simply Vyapar, Zimyo, Agillos, Baldor, Instant Procurement, Mynd Solutions, IB Monotaro, and others) across FY22–FY27, all self-funded, zero debt | FY22–FY27 (ongoing) | Cumulative net balance-sheet growth ₹4,120.28mn in Long-Term Investments FY22→FY26 (individual checks mostly single-digit-to-low-double-digit $mm) | Access to adjacent MSME-tech categories (fleet management, HR SaaS, legal tech, procurement, accounting/vyapar software) | **Mixed, with disclosed losses.** Livekeeping Private Limited (a bolt-on under the Busy umbrella) was struck off in October 2024; Ten Times Online Private Limited was divested in 2023 for a nominal $0.01mm after being acquired for a nominal sum in 2020 (a round-trip loss); a ₹23.81mn impairment and a ₹12.04mn diminution provision were recorded against these investments in FY2025. No disclosed exit has generated a return above cost in the pool. | Real, and compounding: management attention spread across ~15+ small, largely unproven positions, none of which has been publicly reported as a value-creating exit; this is the recurring pattern the §24/Filter-4 opportunity-cost test targets even though no single check is individually material | [Transaction Summary M&A/Private Placements, CIQ export, 10-yr history]; [`business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md`, Section 1 — Livekeeping strike-off, Ten Times Online divestiture, FY25 impairment/diminution figures] |

**Serial-acquirer + opportunity-cost test (CLAUDE.md §24, Filter 4).** IndiaMART made roughly 20 M&A/private-placement deals over the 10-year CIQ history — a genuine serial pattern by count. It clears the two conditions that keep it out of the hard cap: every deal (including Busy Infotech) was funded from cash with **zero debt drawn**, and the one control deal is ~4.7% of the company's own market value, not "near or above" it. `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` scored this row 58/100 (inverted; below the ≥70 threshold), so the RF-CAP-004 hard cap (Capital allocation max 50) is **not** triggered. But "not hitting the hard cap" is not the same as "capital allocated well": Busy Infotech is unproven on a segment-EBITDA basis three years post-close, and the venture portfolio's only disclosed exits to date are a strike-off, a nominal-value divestiture, and two write-down provisions — real capital and real management bandwidth committed with, so far, no evidenced value-creating outcome. This is charged in full to the M&A discipline score below (Section "Capital Allocation Score"), consistent with the CLAUDE.md §24/Filter-4 instruction to price the opportunity cost, not just the absence of a debt-funded disaster. **RF-CAP-004 is not formally triggered (below the ≥70 severity threshold and no debt-funded near-value deal), but the pattern is flagged for continued monitoring** — a fourth or fifth control-scale deal, or a first debt-funded deal, would change this read.

## 3. Buyback Scorecard

Intrinsic-value discipline not assessable from this module (the valuation module has not yet run in this pipeline). Judgment below rests only on the observable evidence MODULE_RULES specifies: net share-count change, the tender-offer process and disclosed price, whether leverage rose alongside the buyback (it did not — the company is and was debt-free throughout), and whether the reduction was offset by ESOP dilution.

| Year | Amount Spent (incl. buyback tax + costs) | Currency | Shares Bought | Implied/Disclosed Price | Share-Count Change | Offset SBC Dilution? | Price-Discipline Evidence | Judgment | Source |
|---|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|---|---|---|---|
| FY23 (announced Apr-2022, closed 15-Jun-2022) | 1,232.6 | INR mn | ~160,000 (nominal, pre-bonus; computed as ₹1,000mn base ÷ ₹6,250 — **inference, not from a specific filing note**, since the FY23 annual report is not in this data pool) | ₹6,250/share nominal (pre-1:1-bonus); **≈₹3,125/share on a bonus-adjusted-equivalent basis** | Total Common Stock (face value) rose FY22→FY23 (305.53→305.79, +0.26mn face-value units), i.e. ESOP issuance more than offset this buyback's share reduction in gross filing terms | Yes — the buyback's own reduction was outrun by ESOP issuance the same year | No contemporaneous 52-week trading-range data is available in this pool for Apr–Jun 2022 to judge the price against the range; the deal was a SEBI tender offer with pro-rata promoter participation, standard process, no evidence of a related-party-favorable price | **Not assessable on price; process was standard; net share-count effect was negative that year (ESOP additions exceeded the buyback)** | [Q4 FY22 earnings-call transcript, Apr 29, 2022 — "buyback at a price of INR 6,250 per share for amounts not exceeding INR 100 crore"]; [Q1 FY23 transcript, Jul 22, 2022 — "completing INR 100 crores share buyback"]; [Financials.xls, Balance Sheet tab, "Common Stock" FY22/FY23] |
| FY24 (announced Jul-2023, closed 25-Sep-2023) | 6,198.84 (per filing note; CIQ Cash Flow tab shows 6,161.9 — a small vendor-vs-filing variance, both cited) | INR mn | 1,250,000 (12,50,000 shares, disclosed exactly) | ₹4,000/share (post-1:1-bonus — the bonus preceded this buyback, so no adjustment needed) | Shares outstanding fell by the 1.25mn extinguished, but the same fiscal year also saw the 1:1 bonus issue (+30.6mn) and ESOP additions (+53,000) — net effect dominated by the bonus, not comparable to the buyback in isolation | Partially — ESOP additions (53,000 shares) were far smaller than the 1.25mn retired this specific year | Tender offer, "12,50,000 equity shares… for an aggregate amount not exceeding INR 5,000 [crore-equivalent nominal], being 2.04% of the total paid-up equity share capital," pro-rata promoter participation disclosed | **Transparent process, fully disclosed price and share count in the annual report; no evidence either way that ₹4,000/share was cheap or expensive absent a trading-range benchmark** | [FY25 Annual Report, Note 2 to Equity — "During the year ended 31 March 2024… buy-back upto 12,50,000 equity shares… at 4,000 per equity share… cash outflow of INR 6,198.84 (including transaction costs of INR 36.95 and tax on buyback of INR 1,161.89)"]; [Q1 FY24 transcript, Jul 21, 2023]; [Q2 FY24 transcript, Oct 27, 2023 — "total pay out of INR 620 crores"] |
| FY25 | Nil | — | 0 | — | — | — | No buyback despite ₹28.4bn+ net cash (broad basis) on the balance sheet at FY25-end | **No capital returned via buyback this year despite a growing treasury book** | [Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab, "Repurchase of Common Stock" FY25 = blank/nil] |
| FY26 | Nil | — | 0 | — | — | — | No buyback despite ₹31.2bn net cash (broad basis) at FY26-end; company instead used a special dividend (₹30/share on top of the ₹30/share final) | **No capital returned via buyback this year; the special dividend substituted for it** | [Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab, "Repurchase of Common Stock" FY26 = blank/nil]; [Key Developments.xls — Special Dividend Announced Apr-30-2026] |

**Net share-count read (bonus-adjusted, the only clean comparable basis given the FY24 bonus):** Capital IQ's own bonus-adjusted share-count series shows Total Shares Outstanding falling from **61.129148mn (FY22)** to **60.082656mn (FY26)** — a net reduction of **~1.05mn shares (~1.7%)** over the 5 years [Financials.xls, Balance Sheet tab, "Total Shares Out. on Filing Date"]. This is a genuine, if modest, net reduction — **RF-CAP-002 ("buybacks not reducing share count") is not triggered** — but it is small relative to the ₹7,394.5mn of gross capital spent on the two buybacks, because ongoing ESOP issuance (stock-based compensation ran ₹102.73mn–₹366.2mn/year, Cash Flow tab) continuously replaced a meaningful share of what the buybacks retired. No buyback has run since September 2023, despite the treasury book growing by a further ~₹8.6bn (broad-basis net cash FY24 ₹22,824.8mn → FY26 ₹30,971.6mn) in the interim — capital accumulating rather than being returned or deployed.

## 4. Dividends & Organic Reinvestment

| Metric | Value | Source |
|---|---:|---|
| Payout ratio, cash-paid basis (Common Dividends Paid ÷ Net Income, same fiscal year) | FY22 15.3%, FY23 2.1%, FY24 18.3%, FY25 21.8%, FY26 63.2% | Computed from [Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab, "Common Dividends Paid"; Cash Flow tab, "Net Income"] |
| Payout ratio, declared basis (FY26 final ₹30 + special ₹30 = ₹60/share, ~₹3,601.9mn, against FY26 declared-year Net Income ₹4,746.82mn) | 75.9% | [Key Developments.xls — Special Dividend Announced Apr-30-2026, AGM Notice Jun-29-2026]; [Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab, Net Income FY26] |
| Dividend coverage (FCF ÷ dividend paid, cash-paid basis, same fiscal year) | FY22 8.7x, FY23 75.2x, FY24 8.9x, FY25 5.1x, FY26 2.3x | Computed from [`earnings/01_historical-financials.md`, Section 1, FCF row]; [Financials.xls, Cash Flow tab, "Common Dividends Paid"] |
| Dividend coverage (FCF ÷ dividend declared, FY26 declared basis) | 1.9x (FY26 FCF ₹6,872.19mn ÷ declared dividend ₹3,601.9mn) | Same sources |
| Incremental ROIC (Δ NOPAT / Δ invested capital), FY22→FY26 | **Not meaningfully computable — the arithmetic returns a negative denominator.** Approximate NOPAT (EBIT × (1 − 25% assumed effective tax rate — *inference, not from filings*): FY22 ₹2,219mn → FY26 ₹3,762mn, ΔNOPAT ≈ +₹1,543mn. Approximate operating invested capital (Total Equity + Total Debt − Cash & ST Investments, stripping the treasury book): FY22 −₹4,470.06mn → FY26 −₹6,967.88mn, ΔInvested Capital ≈ **−₹2,497.82mn** (capital became MORE negative, i.e. the business released capital as it grew). Dividing a positive ΔNOPAT by a negative ΔInvested Capital produces a mathematically defined but economically meaningless negative "ROIC." | Computed from [Financials.xls, Balance Sheet tab, "Total Common Equity," "Total Debt," "Total Cash & ST Investments," FY22 & FY26]; [`earnings/01_historical-financials.md`, Section 1, EBIT row] |

**Reading the incremental-ROIC result plainly, in place of a fabricated number:** IndiaMART's core marketplace requires close to **zero, and structurally negative, operating capital** — customers pay a year or more of subscription upfront (Unearned Revenue, current + non-current, grew from ₹9,070.38mn FY22 to ₹19,652.6mn FY26), there is no inventory, and capex is trivial (₹41.3mn–₹172mn/year against ₹5,015.93mn of FY26 EBIT). This means growth **funds itself and releases cash** rather than consuming it — the standard incremental-ROIC formula is not the right tool here because the denominator is negative, not because the business is capital-inefficient. The correct read is the opposite of inefficiency: every rupee of revenue growth this business has added over the period has come with negative net new capital required, which is a genuinely strong organic-reinvestment characteristic — but it is a qualitative read, not a number that can be defensibly quoted as "X% incremental ROIC," and this report does not manufacture one.

Dividends are well covered in every year shown, including the tightest year (FY26 cash-paid basis, 2.3x FCF coverage); the payout ratio has risen from single digits (FY22–FY23) to a majority of net income (FY26 declared basis, 75.9%), reflecting the special dividend used to return the swollen treasury book rather than fresh reinvestment or a resumed buyback.

---

## 5. Capital-Allocation Read

Capital has been allocated with real per-share discipline in some respects and real drift in others — this is a **mixed** record, not a clean story either way. The best use of capital has been the core organic business itself: negative operating working capital and near-zero capex mean growth has been self-funding, throwing off cash (CFO rose every single year, FY22 ₹4,023.14mn to FY26 ₹6,942.19mn) without requiring net new capital — a genuinely strong, if not cleanly quantifiable, per-share characteristic. Dividends have been consistently well covered and have scaled up appropriately (from 15% of net income in FY22 to a 76% payout including a special dividend in FY26) as cash piled up. The worst use of capital is the acquisition/venture-investing program: the one control deal (Busy Infotech, $66.93mm) has grown revenue sharply but has posted a **negative segment EBITDA for three straight years**, and the ~19 minority venture checks have produced, on the record available, only a strike-off, a nominal-value divestiture, and two write-down provisions — real capital and real founder/management attention committed for an as-yet-unproven return. The single biggest capital-allocation signal is the combination of a swollen, growing treasury book (net cash on the broad basis nearly doubling from ₹22.8bn at FY24-end to ₹31.0bn at FY26-end) with **no buyback since September 2023** — capital is accumulating in mutual funds and bonds rather than being returned to shareholders or redeployed, even as the company continues writing small checks into unrelated MSME-tech startups. Verdict: **mixed**.

## Capital Allocation Score

| Component | Score | Max Score | Evidence |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Organic reinvestment returns | 20 | 25 | Structurally negative operating working capital and trivial capex (₹41–172mn/yr vs ₹5,015.93mn FY26 EBIT) mean growth is self-funding and releases cash rather than consuming it (rows 02-012, 02-013) — a genuinely strong characteristic, docked for decelerating top-line growth (13.02% FY26 vs 30.78% FY23, per `earnings/01_historical-financials.md`) reducing the absolute scale of value created and for the metric not being cleanly quantifiable |
| M&A discipline | 10 | 20 | All-cash, zero-debt, individually small deals avoid the hard §24/Filter-4 cap (row 02-003, 02-006), but the one control deal (Busy Infotech) has posted negative segment EBITDA for 3 straight years post-close (row 02-004) and the ~19-position venture portfolio's only disclosed exits are a strike-off, a nominal-loss divestiture, and two write-downs (row 02-005) — real capital and management attention committed with, so far, no evidenced value-creating outcome |
| Buyback discipline | 11 | 20 | Two buybacks executed via a transparent SEBI tender-offer process with fully disclosed prices and share counts (rows 02-007, 02-008); net share count did fall modestly (row 02-009) but ESOP issuance ate into much of the gross reduction, and no buyback has run since Sep-2023 despite the treasury book growing ~₹8.6bn in the interim (row 02-010) — capital accumulating rather than being returned |
| Dividend sustainability | 13 | 15 | Payout has scaled appropriately from 15% (FY22) to 76% declared (FY26) as cash accumulated; even the tightest year is covered 2.3x by FCF (row 02-011); no coverage concern in any year shown |
| Balance-sheet prudence | 10 | 10 | Debt-free (only Ind AS 116 lease liabilities); every capital-allocation action over the 5-year window was funded from operating cash flow with zero debt drawn (row 02-013) |
| Per-share value creation | 6 | 10 | Diluted EPS rose from ₹48.42 (FY22) to ₹78.77 (FY26), but `earnings/01_historical-financials.md` Section 3 shows this growth leans heavily on volatile, non-operating treasury-investment gains (₹1,038mn–₹2,669mn/year) rather than pure operating execution, and revenue-growth deceleration plus the unproven M&A/venture record temper the read |
| **Total** | **70** | **100** | Strong / low-risk band (61–80), at the lower end: an excellent capital-light organic model and a debt-free, well-covered dividend record, offset by a genuinely mixed and opportunity-cost-laden M&A/venture program and buybacks that have stalled for over two years despite a growing idle-cash pile |

No partial-data cap applies (5 full fiscal years of history are available). No RF-CAP-002 or RF-CAP-003 is triggered. RF-CAP-004 is flagged for monitoring but not hard-capped (severity 58/100, below the ≥70 threshold, consistent with `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md`'s independent assessment).

# Incentives & Compensation — INDIAMART

**Jurisdiction / regime:** India, listed NSE/BSE. Compensation disclosure source: FY2026 Integrated Annual Report (Board's Report — "Remuneration of Directors," Nomination & Remuneration Policy summary, Section 197 disclosure) — the India equivalent of a US DEF 14A Compensation Discussion & Analysis (CLAUDE.md §27). No standalone "proxy statement" exists in this jurisdiction; the Board's Report + Corporate Governance Report disclosure is the canonical source and is used throughout. Cross-module input: `01_management-and-track-record.md` (executive tenure/ownership context).

## 1. Compensation Structure

Reporting currency: **INR (₹)**. Period: **FY2026 (year ended 31-Mar-2026)**, as disclosed in the Integrated Annual Report 2025-26 filed 2026-06-02, unless noted.
