|---|---:|---:|---|---|---|
| Dinesh Chandra Agarwal, Managing Director & CEO (founder) | ₹5,96,12,268 | ₹2,37,07,856 | **None — not eligible.** Founder-promoters are explicitly excluded from the ESOP scheme: "Managing Director & CEO and Whole-time Director, also being Promoters of the Company are not entitled to participate in the ESOP Scheme of the Company." FY26 grant record confirms Nil ESOP/SAR units to the CEO. | 100% cash (₹8,33,20,124 total). Fixed = 71.5% of total; PLVC = 28.5%. | FY26 Annual Report, Board's Report, "Remuneration to Executive Director"; FY26 Annual Report, Note (Employee-wise ESOP/SAR grants), p.~ESOP Annexure |
| Brijesh Kumar Agrawal, Co-Founder & Whole-Time Director | ₹4,34,03,016 | ₹1,83,29,208 | **None — not eligible** (same promoter exclusion as CEO; Nil ESOP/SAR granted FY26). | 100% cash (₹6,17,32,224 total). Fixed = 70.3%; PLVC = 29.7%. | Same as above |
| Manoj Bhargava, Group General Counsel, Company Secretary & Whole-Time Director (non-founder) | ₹1,31,54,362 | ₹33,09,278 | ESOP 2018: 5,770 options outstanding at FY26-end (712 exercised during the year, 0 new grants FY26); vesting is time-based, minimum 1 year / maximum 5 years, exercise price set by the Nomination & Remuneration Committee (NRC) — a service-vesting instrument, not disclosed as performance-hurdle-linked. | ~98% cash / ~2% equity by count of pay components (₹1,64,63,640 cash total, excludes ESOP perquisite value which is not separately quantified in ₹ in this disclosure). | FY26 Annual Report, "Stock Options held by Executive Director" table |
| Jitin Diwan, Chief Financial Officer (KMP, not a Board director — separately disclosed under Section 197(12)) | Not broken out separately in the pool (total below includes fixed + PLVC, undisclosed split) | Included in total; footnote confirms "includes PLVC received for the relevant period of three quarters of FY 2026 and payable for the last quarter of FY 2026" | **13,330 SAR units granted during FY26** (the only KMP other than the Compliance Officer, 1,090 units, to receive a fresh equity grant that year) — quantity disclosed, ₹ grant-date fair value not separately quantified in the pool for this individual. | Total cash+equity FY26 = ₹1,78,83,763 (fixed/bonus split not separately disclosed for the CFO in the reviewed pool) | FY26 Annual Report, Section 197(12) "Ratio of Remuneration" table; FY26 Annual Report, "Employee-wise details of ESOP Options/SAR Units granted during the year" |

**Company-wide KMP compensation aggregate (all KMP combined, FY26 vs FY25, ₹ million):** Short-term employee benefits ₹184.56mn (FY25: ₹168.27mn); post-employment benefits ₹1.11mn; other long-term benefits ₹1.20mn; **employee share-based payment ₹16.11mn** (FY25: ₹12.65mn) — equity-settled pay is ~7.9% of total KMP compensation (₹202.98mn) company-wide, and that share-based slice is earned entirely by non-founder KMP, since the two founders are ineligible. [FY26 Annual Report, Notes to Consolidated Financial Statements, "Key management personnel compensation"]

**Non-Executive/Independent Directors** receive sitting fees only (₹6,00,000–₹19,00,000 in FY26 per director) — "the Company has not provided any other benefits such as bonus and pension neither granted any Employee Stock Options / Stock Appreciation Rights to any of its Non-Executive Directors" [FY26 Annual Report, Board's Report]. A separate, undrawn commission pool for NEDs is authorized up to "1% of the profit of the Company computed as per the applicable provisions of the Act," but no commission was actually paid to any NED in FY26 (all NED rows show sitting fees only, no "Commission" column entry) [FY26 Annual Report, Board's Report, "Criteria of making Payment to Non-Executive Directors"].

## 2. Performance Metrics

| Plan | Metric | Weight | Per-Share/Returns or Size-Based? | Source |
|---|---|---:|---|---|
| Annual bonus ("Performance Linked Incentive / Commission") — Executive Directors | **"Balance Score Card"** (management's own term, as reproduced verbatim) — "based on their performance measured by their Balance Score Card for the previous financial year as recommended by the Nomination and Remuneration Committee and approved by the Board of Directors" | **Not disclosed.** No individual KPI names, target levels, or weights for the Balanced Scorecard are disclosed anywhere in the FY26 Board's Report, Corporate Governance Report, or NRC-policy summary found in the pool. | **Cannot be classified from public disclosure — this is itself the finding.** The scorecard could contain per-share metrics (EPS, FCF/share, ROIC/ROCE) or size metrics (revenue, EBITDA, supplier count) or a blend; the company simply does not say. Given `01_management-and-track-record.md`'s finding that public commentary centers on revenue growth, EBITDA margin, and paying-supplier net-adds (all of which are size/scale or margin metrics, not per-share or returns metrics), it is plausible but **not proven** that the scorecard weights similarly toward growth/margin rather than ROIC or TSR — flagged as inference, not confirmed from filings. | FY26 Annual Report, Board's Report, "Remuneration to Executive Director" |
| Long-term incentive — ESOP/SAR (non-founder KMP and employees only) | Service condition (time-based vesting, 1–5 years); options priced and vesting schedule set at NRC discretion. Ind AS 102 language in the notes ("service and non-market performance conditions") describes standard option-expense accounting, not a disclosed company-specific performance hurdle. | Not applicable — no disclosed performance weighting, only time-vesting. | **Per-share by construction of the instrument** (an ESOP/SAR's payoff is the stock-price appreciation over the exercise price — inherently a per-share value metric), but **not conditioned on any specific per-share financial target** (no disclosed ROIC/EPS/FCF hurdle) — closer to "pay for holding" than "pay for hitting a per-share goal." | FY26 Annual Report, "Stock Options held by Executive Director"; ESOP 2018/2022 and SAR scheme disclosures |
| Non-Executive Director commission (authorized, unused in FY26) | Statutory cap: "not exceeding 1% of the profit of the Company computed as per the applicable provisions of the Act" | Cap only — no scorecard | Size-based ceiling (tied to absolute standalone profit, not per-share profit), but functions as a protective ceiling for minority holders rather than an incentive lever since it went unused in FY26 | FY26 Annual Report, Board's Report |

**No formal, weighted rubric is disclosed for either plan.** This is a genuine disclosure gap, not a language-translation issue (the document is in English) — the company names the mechanism ("Balance Score Card") but not its contents. Per CLAUDE.md §3/§6, absence of the metric detail is recorded as "not proven from available data," not assumed to be either favorable or unfavorable.

## 3. Pay vs Performance

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| CEO total pay (latest, FY26) | ₹8,33,20,124 (~₹8.33 crore), **+11.17% YoY** (footnote: the year-on-year change is driven mainly by the variable PLVC component) | FY26 Annual Report, Section 197(12) table |
| Same-period per-share performance | Diluted EPS fell from ₹91.59 (FY25) to ₹78.77 (FY26), **−14.0%**; the stock price fell from ₹2,331.00 (Apr-29-2025 pricing date, FY25 year-end) to ₹2,103.60 (Apr-30-2026 pricing date, FY26 year-end), **−9.75%** over the same fiscal year | `earnings/01_historical-financials.md`, EPS (diluted) row (CIQ Financials export); CIQ Financials (1).xls, "Historical Capitalization" tab, Share Price row, dated 2025-04-29 and 2026-04-30 |
| Pay-for-performance divergence | **CEO cash pay rose 11.17% in the same fiscal year diluted EPS fell 14.0% and the share price fell 9.75%.** All three non-founder-linked directors' PLVC (which the Board attributes to the Balanced Scorecard) moved in the same direction as the CEO's (all three EDs' total pay rose YoY per the ratio table), i.e. the incentive payout did not fall alongside the per-share metrics that actually declined that year. This is a directly observed, dated divergence — not an inference. | FY26 Annual Report, Section 197(12) table; `earnings/01_historical-financials.md` |
| Pay vs peers (web-sourced, dated 2026-08-13, unverified/estimate) | Info Edge (India) Ltd (Naukri.com's parent, a larger Indian internet listed peer) discloses CEO Hitesh Oberoi's total annual compensation at **~₹2.69 crore** (₹26.93 million) per third-party aggregator data, roughly one-third of IndiaMART's CEO pay of ₹8.33 crore, despite Info Edge's much larger market capitalization (consolidated PAT of ~₹566 crore in a recent quarter alone, per press reports, vs IndiaMART's FY26 full-year consolidated PAT run-rate of roughly ₹473–493 crore per CIQ). On a pay-to-company-size basis, IndiaMART's founder-CEO is paid at a materially higher intensity than this peer. This is Level-2 (reputable web, unverified) evidence per CLAUDE.md §6 and is not from a filing — flagged accordingly, and not load-bearing on its own. | Web: StockLens/aggregator CEO-pay data and press coverage of Info Edge Q4 results, retrieved 2026-08-13 (indicative, unverified) |
| Pay-for-performance alignment (overall read) | **Weak on the evidence available.** The one clean, dated comparison in the data pool (FY26 CEO pay vs FY26 EPS/price) shows pay rising while both per-share metrics fell. Whether the undisclosed Balanced Scorecard would, if published, show a defensible reason for that divergence (e.g., a metric other than EPS/TSR that did improve) cannot be confirmed from the pool. | See rows above |
| Say-on-pay support (%) | **Not disclosed in the reviewed pool.** India does not operate a US-style annual say-on-pay advisory vote; managerial remuneration and NED commission limits are approved by special/ordinary shareholder resolution when first set or revised (the FY26 report references "the resolution approved in item no. 6 of the 25th AGM Notice" as the governing remuneration approval), but the actual voting percentages for that historical resolution are not included in the FY26 Annual Report extract in the pool, and no fresh remuneration resolution was put to shareholders in FY26 (the only FY26 postal-ballot items were an independent-director appointment, approved 99.9968% in favour, and a related-party-transaction approval — neither is a remuneration vote). | FY26 Annual Report, "Details of Remuneration" cross-reference to 25th AGM Notice item 6; FY26 Annual Report, Postal Ballot results table |

## 4. Incentive Alignment Read

The pay structure itself is genuinely opaque on the one question that matters: the "Performance Linked Incentive," which is 28–30% of each founder's cash pay, is explicitly tied to an internal "Balance Score Card" whose metrics and weights are never disclosed — so it cannot be classified as per-share/returns-based or size-based from the filings, and the one dated fact the pool does supply (CEO pay +11.17% in the same year diluted EPS fell 14.0% and the share price fell 9.75%) points toward the payout not tracking per-share outcomes. Working against that: the two founders who set strategy hold no equity compensation at all — they are contractually barred from the ESOP scheme — so whatever alignment exists for them comes entirely from their pre-existing ~49% direct ownership stake (`01_management-and-track-record.md`, Section 1) rather than from any incentive plan a shareholder vote could recalibrate; the only executive with a fresh equity grant in FY26 (CFO, 13,330 SARs) received a time-vested, share-price-linked instrument with no disclosed performance hurdle. Net: this is not a size-based empire-building pay plan in the classic sense (no metric rewards revenue, deal count, or headcount that is disclosed), but it is also not demonstrably a per-share pay plan — it is an **undisclosed** plan that just missed its own most obvious sanity check this year. Verdict: **mixed**, leaning toward the opaque/misaligned side of that band on the pay-structure evidence alone (the founders' ownership stake is a separate, real alignment force this report does not credit to the compensation plan).

## Incentive Alignment Score

| Component | Score | Max Score | Evidence |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Returns / per-share metrics | 9 | 25 | CFO's SAR grant is inherently share-price-linked (row 03-003), but the annual bonus (28–30% of founder cash pay) has an entirely undisclosed metric basis (row 03-004) and the founders — who set strategy — hold zero equity compensation of any kind (row 03-002) |
| Metric clarity and weights | 4 | 20 | "Balance Score Card" is named but never itemized anywhere in the pool across FY22–FY26; no KPI list, no weights, no target bands disclosed for either the annual bonus or the (time-vesting only) LTIP (row 03-004, 03-005) |
| Pay-for-performance alignment | 4 | 20 | The one clean, dated comparison available shows CEO cash pay +11.17% in the same fiscal year diluted EPS fell 14.0% and the share price fell 9.75% (rows 03-006, 03-007) — a direct divergence, not an inference |
| Long-term orientation | 6 | 15 | LTIP exists only for non-founder KMP, is time-vested (1–5 years) rather than performance-vested, and the annual bonus is explicitly assessed "for the previous financial year" only — i.e., single-year lookback, no multi-year performance period disclosed |
| Pay magnitude vs peers/performance | 3 | 10 | CEO pay is roughly 3x a larger domestic peer's disclosed CEO pay on a single-peer, web-sourced comparison (row 03-008, Level 2 evidence, not load-bearing alone); combined ED pay is a modest ~3.3% of consolidated PAT (row 03-011), a partial offset |
| Minority-holder alignment | 6 | 10 | The unused 1%-of-profit NED commission cap and the founders' ~49% direct ownership (carried outside this pay plan) are real protective/alignment features, but they sit outside the compensation *structure* being scored here, and no shareholder vote on remuneration occurred in FY26 to test investor sentiment directly (row 03-009, Insufficient Data) |
| Total | 32 | 100 | Weak band (21–40, CLAUDE.md §12): an opaque bonus-metric design that, on the only year checkable in the pool, paid the CEO more while diluted EPS and the share price both fell; partially offset by founders' large pre-existing ownership stake, which sits outside the incentive plan itself, and by a modest overall pay-to-profit ratio |

No blanket partial-data cap applies (a proxy-equivalent compensation disclosure does exist and was read in full), but the Metric-clarity and Pay-for-performance components are individually capped low by a genuine, cited disclosure gap (the undisclosed Balanced Scorecard) rather than by absence of any disclosure at all.

## management-governance / 04_ownership-and-insider-behavior.md

_Source: `04_ownership-and-insider-behavior.md`_

# Ownership & Insider Behavior — INDIAMART

Jurisdiction: India (NSE: INDIAMART), SEBI-LODR regime. Ownership source is the shareholding-pattern table in the Corporate Governance Report (the Indian proxy-equivalent), cross-checked against the Capital IQ Public Ownership Summary export. Insider-transaction detail is limited: no dedicated SAST/PIT (Prohibition of Insider Trading) transaction-disclosure filing or exchange-intimation log is in the data pool, so per-transaction insider trading history rests on the CIQ ownership-flow table (a data-vendor export, not a filing) plus two Secretarial Audit compliance-lapse mentions. That gap is called out explicitly below and caps the "recent insider behavior" score component to low confidence.

## 1. Ownership

| Holder | % Held | Share Class | Bought or Granted? | Source |
|---|---:|---|---|---|
| CEO / founder — Dinesh Chandra Agarwal (CEO, MD & Executive Director since Sep-13-1999) | 29.09% (17,492,084 shares) | Ordinary equity, single class | Bought — founding equity from company incorporation in 1999, not an option/RSU grant | [FY26 Annual Report (Jun-02-2026), Corporate Governance Report, "A. Promoters Holdings," p.7219]; [CIQ Public Ownership Summary, Top Holders, position date Mar-31-2026] |
| Co-founder — Brijesh Kumar Agrawal (Whole-Time Director since Sep-13-1999) | 19.99% (12,022,004 shares) | Ordinary equity, single class | Bought — founding equity from 1999 incorporation | [FY26 Annual Report, Corporate Governance Report, p.7219]; [CIQ Public Ownership Summary, Top Holders, Mar-31-2026] |
| Promoter & Promoter Group (total, 18 holders — includes the two founders plus family/promoter-group entities) | 49.17% (2,95,14,888 shares) | Ordinary equity | Bought at founding for the two named founders; basis for the remaining 16 promoter-group holders not itemized in the pool | [FY26 Annual Report, Corporate Governance Report, "A. Promoters Holdings," p.7219] |
| Largest institutional holder — Nalanda Capital Pte Ltd | 7.71% (4,636,599 shares) | Ordinary equity | Bought (open-market institutional purchase) | [CIQ Public Ownership Summary, Top Holders, position date Jul-21-2026] |
| ICICI Prudential Asset Management Company Limited | 6.47% (3,891,506 shares) | Ordinary equity | Bought (fund purchase) | [CIQ Public Ownership Summary, Top Holders, Mar-31-2026] |
| UTI Asset Management Company Limited | 3.48% (2,091,031 shares) | Ordinary equity | Bought (fund purchase) | [CIQ Public Ownership Summary, Top Holders, Mar-31-2026] |
| Total institutions (all, aggregate) | 25.48% (15,320,520 shares); 51 holders | Ordinary equity | Bought | [CIQ Public Ownership Summary, Institutional Ownership Details, Mar-31-2026] |
| Corporations (private) | 1.65% (992,122 shares) | Ordinary equity | Not itemized | [CIQ Public Ownership Summary, Mar-31-2026] |
| Public and other (retail + unclassified) | 14.72% (8,851,607 shares) | Ordinary equity | Not itemized | [CIQ Public Ownership Summary, Mar-31-2026] |

**Total insider ownership: 49.17%** on the audited Promoter & Promoter Group basis (Corporate Governance Report, the higher-tier source per CLAUDE.md §4). A separately-sourced CIQ vendor classification, "Individuals/Insiders," reports 50.44% (30,332,710 shares, 9 holders) — about 1.27 percentage points above the audited promoter figure. That gap is not reconciled in the pool (the CIQ category likely nets in a small number of non-promoter individual insiders alongside the promoter group); it is reported here as a labeled discrepancy, not resolved, and the audited 49.17% is used as the governing figure per the source hierarchy.

The two founders alone hold 49.08% directly — almost the entire insider block — and both remain full-time executive directors 27 years after founding the company. This is genuine skin in the game: the stake originates from founding equity at incorporation (1999), not from options, RSUs, or other post-listing grants. No evidence in the pool shows either founder making incremental open-market purchases since; the stake has been static and inherited-in-place rather than actively added to.

## 2. Insider Transactions (last 12 months)

| Insider | Buy / Sell | Amount | Date | Notes (pre-planned trading window?) | Source |
|---|---|---:|---|---|---|
| Unidentified insiders, CIQ "Individuals/Insiders" aggregate | Sell (decreased positions) | 2 holders, −18,872 shares (immaterial, <0.1% of the 30.3mn-share insider block) | Dec-31-2025 to Mar-31-2026 | Individual identities not disclosed in this vendor table | [CIQ Public Ownership Summary, "Ownership Activity — Individuals/Insiders," Mar-31-2026] |
| Agrawal, Madhup (promoter-group-linked individual holder; 779,792 shares held) | Sell | −18,072 shares (change) | Dec-31-2025 to Mar-31-2026 (window) | Not identified as a 10b5-1-equivalent pre-planned sale in the pool; magnitude is ~2.3% of his own holding, immaterial to the company | [CIQ Public Ownership Summary, "Top Buyers and Sellers," data as of Aug-11-2026] |
| Unidentified insiders, CIQ "Individuals/Insiders" aggregate | Buy | 2 transactions, 513,115 shares | May-13-2026 to Aug-13-2026 | No individual names, no pre-planned-trading-window flag available | [CIQ Public Ownership Summary, "Ownership Activity — Individuals/Insiders," data as of Aug-13-2026] |

**Net insider activity: net buying** in the most recent (May–Aug 2026) window on the CIQ flow table (2 buy transactions, 513,115 shares, zero disclosed sells), following a small, immaterial net decrease in the prior quarter. **This read carries low confidence and is explicitly capped, not a red-flag basis:** the pool contains no dedicated SAST/PIT (Prohibition of Insider Trading) transaction-disclosure filing or per-trade exchange intimation — the only source is a Capital IQ data-vendor ownership-flow table (a tier-5 source per CLAUDE.md §4, below any filing), which aggregates transactions without naming the individual insider, the price paid, or whether a trade was a pre-planned/pre-cleared transaction. No open-market buy can be distinguished from an option exercise or an inter-family transfer on this data. Per the module's red-flag gating rule, this vendor-sourced signal is reported as labeled context only and does not by itself support or refute any RF-OWN or RF-MKT red flag.

## 3. Pledging & Encumbrance

| Insider | Shares Pledged | % of Their Holding | Source |
|---|---:|---:|---|
| Promoter & Promoter Group (aggregate) | Not disclosed | Not disclosed | [FY26 Annual Report, Corporate Governance Report, "A. Promoters Holdings," p.7219 — the shareholding-pattern table carries no pledge/encumbrance column in this filing] |
| Unnamed "Designated Person" (individual-level, seniority not disclosed) | Pledge created; size not quantified | Not disclosed | [FY26 Annual Report, Secretarial Audit Report, Annexure-A] |

No promoter-level pledge percentage is disclosed anywhere in the pool. The one pledge-related fact is an audited Secretarial Audit Report finding — an unnamed Designated Person "created pledge on equity shares of the Company without seeking pre-clearance from the Compliance Officer," self-reported by the company to the exchanges [FY26 Annual Report, Secretarial Audit Report, Annexure-A]. This is a **process/pre-clearance compliance lapse**, not evidence that the pledge was large, that it breached a ceiling, or that shares were concealed — the pledge itself was disclosed to the exchanges once made. It does not meet the evidence bar to fire RF-OWN-001 (promoter pledge above threshold) or RF-OWN-002 (pledge increased QoQ) because no pledge magnitude is disclosed to test against any threshold; `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md` independently reached the same conclusion and computed the disqualifier ratio as "not disclosed / not computable — treated as ~0%," well under the 50% hard-disqualifier threshold [`business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md`, Section on disqualifier #2]. A second, unrelated Designated Person compliance item — a one-share trade during a closed trading window — is immaterial by definition and not a pledge issue.

**Disclosure-completeness note:** SEBI shareholding-pattern filings generally carry a pledge/encumbrance column; its absence here from the reviewed pool means the analysis cannot independently confirm a true 0% promoter pledge — it can only confirm that no non-zero pledge magnitude is disclosed. This is flagged as a residual data gap, not treated as proof of a clean pledge position.

## 4. Control Structure

| Feature | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Dual-class / super-voting | None found. Single class of ordinary equity, one vote per share. | [FY26 Annual Report, Corporate Governance Report, shareholding pattern; `management-governance/00_governance-data-triage.md`] |
| Controlled-company status | Not a formally "controlled company" under a >50% single-block test — Promoter & Promoter Group hold 49.17%, just under a bare majority — but the two founders individually hold board seats as CEO/MD and Whole-Time Director continuously since 1999, giving practical operating control regardless of the sub-50% vote share. | [FY26 Annual Report, Corporate Governance Report, p.7219; Board Members export] |
| Shareholder bloc with board rights | No institutional shareholder (Nalanda Capital 7.71%, ICICI Prudential AMC 6.47%, UTI AMC 3.48%) is disclosed as holding a board-nomination or veto right in the pool. | [CIQ Public Ownership Summary; FY26 Annual Report, Corporate Governance Report — no nomination-agreement disclosure found] |
| Controlling-owner objective alignment *(Filter 6)* | Standalone, founder-led, NSE-listed operating company. Not government-controlled, not a listed subsidiary of a value-maximizing parent, and not a sprawling unrelated-diversified conglomerate (the company does run a portfolio of ~15+ small minority venture investments, but that is scored under `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md` as a capital-allocation opportunity-cost question, not a control-structure conflict). | [`business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md`; `business-model/11_capital-allocation-governance.md`; FY26 Annual Report] |

**Unaligned-owner test (CLAUDE.md §24, Filter 6): does not apply.** IndiaMART is not government-controlled, is not a listed subsidiary whose parent can steer value to a sibling vehicle, and is not a sprawling unrelated-diversified conglomerate — it is a standalone, single-class-equity, founder-operated company where the two largest shareholders are also the two senior executives running it day to day. RF-OWN-004 is **not triggered**. This is a materially different risk profile from a government-controlled or parent-subsidiary structure: the controlling owners' economic interest (49.08% of the equity) and their operating decisions are the same two people, which points toward alignment with per-share value creation rather than against it — subject to the execution-quality and succession caveats raised in `01_management-and-track-record.md` (27-year unbroken tenure with zero disclosed succession plan is a key-person risk, not a control-alignment problem).

**Minority-rights context (not a control-structure defect, but relevant to how the promoter bloc is used):** a related-party "office or place of profit" appointment for a promoter-family relative (Amit Agarwal) required a postal-ballot shareholder vote in FY26 and drew **21.72% of votes against** (78.28% in favour) [FY26 Annual Report, p.137, Postal Ballot Result]. The resolution passed comfortably, but a vote-against level in the low-20s on a promoter-family RPT resolution is a meaningful minority-shareholder pushback signal and is noted here as a fact for the shareholder-friendliness/board-independence agent (`05`) to weigh, not itself an unaligned-owner trigger.

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Insider buys/sells vs subsequent results (timing) | Not assessable from the pool. The only insider-transaction data available is the CIQ quarterly ownership-flow aggregate (no per-trade dates, no matching to earnings-announcement dates); no SAST/PIT filing with named individuals and trade dates was found. | [CIQ Public Ownership Summary — "Ownership Activity" tables only] |
| Unusual price / volume before announcements | Not assessable — no trading-volume-around-events data is in the pool for this agent. | Insufficient Data |
| Exchange clarification sought for a price move | None found in the Key Developments feed reviewed for the trailing 12 months (conference appearances, AGM notice, and dividend declaration are the only INDIAMART-specific entries; no unusual-movement clarification item). | [CIQ Key Developments export] |
| Institutional (FII/DII/MF) holding trend; any sharp high-quality-holder exit | Net institutional buying in the most recent quarter: 54 total institutional holders holding 18,643,108 shares, with 5 new positions (+4,641,152 shares), 21 increased positions (+821,527 shares), 9 decreased positions (−836,113 shares), and 1 sold-out position (−1,222,307 shares, "The Vanguard Group, Inc."). The sold-out Vanguard entity is offset in the same period by a new position from "Vanguard Capital Management, LLC" (+1,230,690 shares) — this reads as an intra-Vanguard reclassification rather than a genuine Vanguard exit. Nalanda Capital (+1,245,860 shares) and ICICI Prudential AMC (+697,484 shares) were the largest net institutional buyers; UTI AMC trimmed modestly (−53,427 shares). No sharp, unexplained high-quality-holder exit is evident. | [CIQ Public Ownership Summary, "Ownership Activity — Institutions" and "Top Buyers and Sellers," Dec-31-2025 to Mar-31-2026] |

No insider-selling-ahead-of-weak-results pattern is evidenced (RF-MKT-001 not triggered — the only individual sale identified, Agrawal Madhup's −18,072 shares, is immaterial and not time-matched to any results announcement in the pool). No unusual price/volume signal is available to test (RF-MKT-002 not assessable — Insufficient Data, not "clear").

## 5. Ownership / Insider Read

Skin in the game is real and large: the two founders hold 49.08% of the company directly, bought at founding rather than granted through options, and both still run it as full-time executive directors 27 years later — that is about as aligned an ownership structure as this kind of company gets. The insider-transaction signal is thin rather than alarming: the only data available (a data-vendor flow table, not a filing) shows a small, immaterial insider sale in the prior quarter and a larger, unidentified insider buy in the most recent quarter — reported here as labeled, low-confidence context because no SAST/PIT filing exists in the pool to verify who bought, at what price, or under what plan. The biggest risk to minority holders is not pledging (none is disclosed at the promoter level, only an unquantified individual pre-clearance lapse) or control structure (single-class equity, no dual-class trap) — it is succession: a 49%-owning, 27-year, two-person founder bloc with zero disclosed contingency plan, sitting on top of a 21.72%-against vote on a promoter-family related-party appointment, which is a governance-fairness watch item for the board-quality agent (`05`) to carry forward.

## management-governance / 05_board-and-shareholder-rights.md

_Source: `05_board-and-shareholder-rights.md`_

# Board & Shareholder Rights — INDIAMART

Filing regime: India — SEBI (Listing Obligations and Disclosure Requirements) Regulations 2015 + Companies Act 2013; the proxy-equivalent documents used here are the FY26 Integrated Annual Report's Corporate Governance Report, Board's Report, Secretarial Audit Report, and the FY26 Postal Ballot results (Companies Act ss.108/110) [`IndiaMART_InterMESH_Limited_-_Form_Annual_Report(Jun-02-2026).pdf`]. Cross-module inputs used: `management-governance/01_management-and-track-record.md` (founder tenure, ownership, integrity soft-signal already cleared), `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md` (RPT and pledge disqualifier tests, both not triggered). `management-governance/04_ownership-and-insider-behavior.md` has not run yet in this pipeline order — the ownership-alignment cross-check in Section 5 is based on this agent's own read of the FY26 shareholding pattern, not a completed 04 output. No `ciq_facts.json` sidecar exists for this run; all figures below are read directly from the CIQ Board Members / Committees workbook extracts and the FY26 Integrated Annual Report text extract.

## 1. Board Composition

| Feature | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Board size | 8 directors as of Aug-2026 (current CIQ roster); was 9 as of 31-Mar-2026 (3 Executive, 1 Non-Executive non-independent, 5 Independent) before the non-independent Non-Executive Director (Dhruv Prakash) exited the board effective 29-Jun-2026 | [CIQ Board Members export, current roster; FY26 Annual Report, Corporate Governance Report, "the Company's Board is comprised of nine (9) Directors...as on March 31, 2026"; `IndiaMART_InterMESH_Limited_-_Form_Interim_Report(Jul-21-2026)`, KMP list, "Dhruv Prakash...Non-Executive Director (Upto 29 June 2026)"] |
| Independent directors (%) | 5 of 8 = 62.5% (current); 5 of 9 = 55.6% as of 31-Mar-2026 — both clear the SEBI LODR Reg 17(1)(b) minimum of 50% independent that applies because the company has **no designated Chairperson** | [CIQ Board Members export; FY26 Annual Report, Corporate Governance Report, "at least 50% of its Board members were Independent Directors as on March 31, 2026"] |
| Chair / CEO split? | No designated Chairman of the Board at all — the company states this explicitly. A Non-Executive/Independent Director is elected to chair each individual board meeting on a rotating basis; the Lead Independent Director (Pallavi Dinodia Gupta, since 24-Mar-2023) is the closest fixed governance-lead role. The CEO (Dinesh Chandra Agarwal) never chairs the board. | [FY26 Annual Report, Corporate Governance Report, "There is no designated Chairman of the Board... The Chairman of the Board so elected in every board meeting is a Non-Executive Director/Independent Director and his/her position is separate from that of the Managing Director or Chief Executive Officer"] |
| Audit / Comp / Nominating committee independence | Audit Committee: 3/3 independent (Vivek N. Gour, Sandeep K. Barasia, Pallavi D. Gupta). Nomination & Remuneration Committee (CIQ splits this into "Compensation" + "Nominating," both with identical independent membership): 100% independent, chaired by Gour through 17-Jul-2025 then by Lead Independent Director Gupta from 18-Jul-2025 | [CIQ Committees export; FY26 Annual Report, Committee composition tables, pp.109-111 range and Committee attendance tables] |

## 2. Tenure & Refreshment

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Average independent-director tenure | ~3.2 years across the 5 current independent directors (Gour ~8 yrs since Apr-2018; Gupta ~4 yrs since Oct-2022; Vij ~1.5 yrs since Jan-2025; Barasia ~1.3 yrs since Apr-2025; Vasuta Agarwal ~1 yr since Jul/Aug-2025) — short on average because three of five seats turned over inside the trailing 15 months | [CIQ Board Members export, "Years on Board" field; FY26 Annual Report, KMP appointment-date table] |
| Overboarding / staleness signs | None found. No current independent director exceeds SEBI LODR Reg 17A's board-seat caps: Gour holds the most outside listed seats (MakeMyTrip, Affle 3i, plus IndiaMART = 3 listed boards), well under the regulatory cap of 7 (or 8 including a chairperson exemption). No director's tenure exceeds the two-term/10-year Companies Act s.149(10)-(11) limit for independent directors currently in office | [CIQ Board Members export, "Other Boards" field; FY26 Annual Report, director bios, pp.108-112 range] |
| Recent refreshment | Three new independent directors were added within 15 months: Manish Vij (21-Jan-2025), Sandeep Barasia (29-Apr-2025), Vasuta Agarwal (18-Jul-2025). This refreshment was driven by two departures: (1) Rajesh Sawhney's routine, disclosed exit on 22-Sep-2025 after completing his statutory-maximum second 3-year term (not a concern signal), and (2) Aakash Chaudhry's resignation effective 21-Jan-2025, disclosed reason "personal reasons," with the company's Reg.30 filing confirming "no other material reasons" — a resignation that clears as routine on the disclosed record but is nonetheless the kind of event the Red-Flag Trigger Engine tracks. Net effect: continuity is anchored by the two longest-serving independents (Gour, Gupta), but company-specific institutional memory on the board is thinner than the tenure numbers alone would suggest given 3 of 5 seats are under 18 months old | [`IndiaMART_InterMESH_Limited_-_Form_Preliminary_Interim_Report(Jan-22-2025)`, Reg.30 disclosure "Resignation of Mr. Aakash Chaudhry...due to personal reasons"; FY26 Annual Report, "Mr. Rajesh Sawhney...ceased to be an Independent Director...following the completion of his second term"] |

**Materiality read (per MODULE_RULES thresholds):** the revenue/expense-flow RPTs (purchases 0.72%, sales 0.11%) are Low materiality and consistent with `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan.md`'s finding that the RPT >25%-of-revenue/expenses disqualifier does not trigger. Two items rise above a pure box-tick: the **investments-in-related-parties ratio (26.57% of total investment portfolio, up from 24.24%)** is a capital-allocation concentration signal worth monitoring (it reflects a deliberate strategy of funding group affiliates rather than external instruments — not obviously leakage, but it means over a quarter of the company's investible surplus sits in entities the founders/KMP also influence), and the **Amit Agarwal "place of profit" appointment**, which is a genuine self-dealing channel (family-relative hire requiring an RPT shareholder vote) that a meaningful minority (21.72%) voted against. Neither clears the >10%/Critical bar the disqualifier scan uses, but the Amit Agarwal item is flagged here as **RF-RPT-002** (promoter-linked RPT) and cross-referenced to **RF-SHR-001** (high votes against a key resolution) in Section 4A below.

## 4. Takeover Defenses & Voting Rights

| Feature | Present? | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| Poison pill | No — not available under Indian company law | India has no poison-pill / shareholder-rights-plan mechanism; the functional substitute is SEBI's Substantial Acquisition of Shares and Takeovers (SAST) Regulations, which trigger a mandatory open offer at 25% acquisition and cap creeping acquisition at 5%/year up to 75% — a regulatory, not company-specific, defense | Inference from Indian regulatory framework, not from filings |
| Classified / staggered board | No | Directors retire by rotation — one-third of the rotational directors retire and stand for re-election at every AGM (Companies Act s.152), which is the opposite of a staggered board: it is *easier*, not harder, for shareholders to periodically re-test each director's mandate | [FY26 Annual Report, "one-third of the Directors, as are liable to retire by rotation, shall retire every year"] |
| Dual-class / unequal voting | No | Single class of equity shares, ₹10 face value, one-share-one-vote; no super-voting or founder-class shares disclosed anywhere in the pool | [FY26 Annual Report, Corporate Governance Report, Distribution of Shareholding table, "6,00,87,148 Equity Shares of ₹10 each"] |
| Majority vs plurality director voting | Majority | Indian company law has no plurality-voting concept; ordinary resolutions (including director appointments) require a simple majority of votes cast, special resolutions require 75% | Inference from Companies Act 2013 framework, not from filings |
| Shareholder ability to call meetings / act by written consent | Yes, via statutory mechanisms actively used | Postal ballot was used twice in FY26 (Vasuta Agarwal's appointment and the Amit Agarwal RPT approval), both completed via remote e-voting with published results. Members holding ≥10% of paid-up capital can requisition an Extraordinary General Meeting under Companies Act s.100; no EGM was requisitioned by minority shareholders in FY26 | [FY26 Annual Report, "Postal Ballot" section, "During FY 2026...members of the Company approved two (02) resolutions by way of Postal Ballot"; "During FY 2026, No Extraordinary General Meeting of the members of the Company was convened"] |

## 4A. Shareholder Voting & Dilution

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| AGM / Postal-Ballot votes against (remuneration / RPT / director / auditor resolutions) | Two FY26 Postal Ballot resolutions: (1) Vasuta Agarwal's appointment as Independent Director — 99.9968% FOR / 0.0032% AGAINST (near-unanimous); (2) Amit Agarwal RPT "office or place of profit" appointment — 78.2829% FOR / **21.7171% AGAINST**. The second clears the informal ">20% against" high-opposition band the Red-Flag Trigger Engine watches for on RPT/remuneration resolutions. **RF-SHR-001 triggered** (High votes against a key resolution) at Medium-to-High severity: the amount at stake is small (RPT immaterial in ₹ terms), but the *type* of resolution (a family-relative paid appointment) and the *size* of the dissent (over one-fifth of votes polled) is a genuine minority-alignment signal, not noise | [FY26 Annual Report, Postal Ballot results table] |
| Proxy-advisor recommendations (ISS / Glass Lewis / IiAS) | Not disclosed in the reviewed data pool — no proxy-advisor report or citation found | Insufficient Data — none of the reviewed filings, transcripts, or CIQ exports contain a proxy-advisor recommendation |
| Institutional opposition to any resolution | Not separately broken out by holder class in the disclosed Postal Ballot results (results are aggregate FOR/AGAINST share counts, not itemized by institution). Institutional ownership (FPI 19.17% + Mutual Funds 11.85% + AIFs 1.23% = 32.25% of total shares as of 31-Mar-2026) is large enough that institutional votes plausibly drove a meaningful share of the 21.72% against the Amit Agarwal resolution, but this cannot be confirmed from the pool | [FY26 Annual Report, Categories of shareholders table] |
| Dilution history (preferential allotment, warrants, QIP, ESOP pool) | Modest, disclosed, shareholder-approved. FY26: 13,592 ESOP options + 25,787 SAR units exercised, converting into 77,586 new equity shares — 0.13% of the 60,087,148 shares outstanding at year-end. A 2022 AGM resolution increased the SAR-linked equity pool by 300,000 shares (to a cap of 1,000,000 shares under the scheme) — a small, disclosed, and shareholder-approved ESOP ceiling relative to the ~60.09mn shares outstanding (max potential dilution from the SAR pool ≈1.7% of shares outstanding if fully exercised). No preferential allotment, warrants, or QIP found in the FY26 pool | [FY26 Annual Report, "During FY 2026, all vested 13,592 ESOP options and 25,787 SARs units were duly exercised...resulted in transfer of 77,586 Equity Shares"; "the shareholders in the 23rd Annual General Meeting...approved the increase in the equity pool...by adding 3,00,000...fresh equity shares aggregating to not more than 10,00,000...Equity Shares"] |

## 5. Minority-Shareholder Protection Read

The board clears the mechanical independence tests with room to spare — 62.5% independent, a fully independent audit committee and nomination/remuneration committee, no designated Chairman (so the CEO never chairs board meetings), no staggered board, no dual-class stock, and shareholders have used postal-ballot rights twice in the year under review with results published transparently, including one vote the resolution nearly failed to win comfortably. That machinery is real, not decorative. But two findings temper an otherwise clean read: a sitting Non-Executive Director held a personal paid-services contract with the company for six years running (Dhruv Prakash, ₹47 lakh in FY26 alone, only closed out when he left the board in June 2026), and the board asked shareholders to approve a paid position for a relative of the founder's family (Amit Agarwal) — a resolution that a genuine 21.72% of votes polled opposed, the clearest minority-dissent signal in this year's record. Neither item is large in rupee terms, and neither is undisclosed, but together they show the self-dealing channel that a founder-controlled board with no independent Chairman naturally creates: family and insider appointments still make it onto the agenda, and it took a real minority vote, not board self-policing, to register the objection. **Verdict: adequate** — the structural protections (independence ratios, committee composition, voting rights, absence of anti-takeover devices) are genuinely in place, but the RPT-vote outcome and the now-closed director service-agreement show minority holders had to exercise their rights actively rather than rely on the board alone to screen out insider appointments. This is not "strong protection" (that would require the board itself to have kept the Amit Agarwal appointment off the ballot, or for the vote to have been closer to unanimous), and it is well short of "weak/entrenched" (there is no dual-class stock, no staggered board, no blocked minority voting channel, and the promoter group holds only 49.17% — a minority-plus-institutional bloc of 50.88% retains real voting power if it coordinates). No cross-module `04_ownership-and-insider-behavior` finding of a structurally unaligned controlling owner (RF-OWN-004) exists to test against, since that agent has not yet run in this pipeline order; based on this agent's own read, the controlling group is the two co-founders with real, purchased-at-founding equity, not a government, a value-maximizing parent, or an unrelated conglomerate — so the §24 Filter 6 floor does not apply here.

## management-governance / 06_candor-and-disclosure-quality.md

_Source: `06_candor-and-disclosure-quality.md`_

# Candor & Disclosure Quality — INDIAMART

Cross-module inputs: `01_management-and-track-record.md` (promise-vs-delivery record, Section 4); `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md` (non-GAAP aggressiveness, accrual quality); `earnings/04_guidance-consensus.md` (no formal guidance, beat/miss pattern). No `ciq_facts.json` sidecar exists for this run; figures below are read directly from the verbatim earnings-call transcript extracts in `analyses/INDIAMART_2026-08-13/_pool_extracts/` (CIQ/S&P Global transcripts, eleven quarters spanning FY22–FY27) and the FY26 Integrated Annual Report. This is **not** a partial-data case: the pool contains verbatim (not paraphrased) transcripts, so tone, Q&A-directness, and owning-the-miss are directly assessable — no cap applies from the partial-data rule.

## 1. Owning the Misses

| Miss (from 01) | How It Was Communicated | Owned or Obscured? | Source |
|---|---|---|---|
| FY24 consolidated EBITDA margin: 26.54% actual vs "28% to 29%… reach 30%" guided (Q4 FY23 call) | On the Q4 FY24 call (the call that reported the miss), management gave a specific, numbers-based breakdown of why margin fell short: the annual increment date shift (Dec vs Jan), sales-and-marketing cost per revenue falling from 20% (FY23) to 18% then 17%, and the tail-off of a FY22–FY23 hiring surge. No vague "headwinds" language — the explanation was itemized and checkable. | **Owned**, with specific drivers | `Q4 FY24 transcript, Q&A (Dinesh Agarwal), Apr 30, 2024` |
| FY25 consolidated revenue growth: 16.01% actual vs "33%" consolidated aspiration / "20–25%" standalone (Q4 FY24 call) | Same Q4 FY24 call, live, unprompted concession: *"30% at this juncture on a stand-alone basis sounds a little difficult. But yes, on consolidated basis, I continue to have an aspiration for 33%."* Management also broke the 16–17% collection growth into its two components — 6 percentage points from new-customer additions, 10 points from ARPU — rather than leaving the miss as an unexplained aggregate number. | **Owned**, with a quantified breakdown of the shortfall's source | `Q4 FY24 transcript, Q&A (Dinesh Agarwal), Apr 30, 2024` |
| Paying-supplier net additions: guided "8,000 to 9,000/quarter" (Apr-2024) vs 1,800–2,700/quarter actual (FY25) | On the Q4 FY25 call, when directly pressed on whether the net-add rate would recover, the CEO answered: *"I'm really not confident to give you any answer as of now. I mean I'll take another few quarters when I can give you answer on that side."* This is a blunt admission of not knowing, not a false reassurance or a deflection to an unrelated metric. | **Owned** — candid admission of inability to forecast, rather than a manufactured explanation | `Q4 FY25 transcript, Q&A (Dinesh Agarwal / Nikhil Choudhary), Apr 29, 2025` |
| Core-platform buyer-side funnel stagnation / paying-supplier net decline of 1,850 in Q1 FY27 | The CEO led with this negative number in the **prepared remarks** (not held back until a question forced it out): *"Our total paying supplier base was 218,000 at the end of the quarter 1, reflecting a net decline of 1,850 suppliers during the quarter. This decrease can be primarily attributed to moderation in the gross addition as well as elevated churn at the silver subscription tier."* Two named causes given immediately, not one generic explanation. | **Owned**, disclosed proactively in prepared remarks | `Q1 FY27 transcript, prepared remarks (Dinesh Agarwal), Jul 21, 2026` |
| EPS volatility driven by non-operating treasury mark-to-market swings (Q2 FY26 miss, Q4 FY26 miss) | In both quarters, the CFO/CSO disclosed the driver of the swing in prepared remarks before any analyst asked: Q2 FY26 — *"other income from INR 92 crores in previous quarter to INR 10 crores in this quarter, primarily due to the mark-to-market losses on our treasury portfolio owing to significant [bond yield] increase"*; Q4 FY26 — *"consolidated other income for the quarter stood at negative INR 34 crores. This was primarily driven by mark-to-market losses on our treasury portfolio resulting from a significant increase in bond [yields]."* | **Owned**, disclosed unprompted before the Q&A even opened | `Q2 FY26 transcript, prepared remarks (Jitin Diwan), Oct 17, 2025`; `Q4 FY26 transcript, prepared remarks (Jitin Diwan), Apr 30, 2026` |

**One residual gap, not an obscuring pattern but a genuine transparency shortfall:** the net-add guidance was withdrawn on the Q4 FY25 call (Apr-2025) and, per the Q1 FY27 call (Jul-2026), still has no replacement forward number over a year later — management continues to discuss the *raw* net-add figure candidly each quarter (see Section 3) but has not resumed giving investors any forward view on the metric it once guided most specifically. Cross-referenced to `01_management-and-track-record.md`'s RF-MGT-003 finding (growth-target walked down 33% → 20–25% → <10% over four quarters): the pattern across all of Section 1 is that management is candid about *what happened* and *why*, quarter by quarter, even as the multi-quarter arc of targets being set, missed, and cut looks poor on a promise-vs-delivery basis (that arc is `01`'s finding, not re-scored here — this section is about the honesty of the *communication*, not the quality of the *result*).

## 2. Non-GAAP / Adjustment Aggressiveness

| Signal | Detail | Source |
|---|---|---|
| Recurring "one-off" addbacks | **None as a formal adjusted-earnings metric** — IndiaMART reports a single set of Ind AS GAAP figures; a full-text search of the FY26 Annual Report found no "adjusted EBITDA," "non-GAAP," or "adjusted EPS" language anywhere. This is the cleanest possible read on formal non-GAAP discipline. **However**, on earnings calls management repeatedly labels specific investment-revaluation gains as "onetime" — each instance tied to a discrete, named event (ProcMart's fundraise, Q3 FY23 and again Q4 FY24; Baldor Technologies' revaluation, Q3 FY26) — while the broader treasury mark-to-market line (mutual funds/ETFs/bonds) is usually described factually without the "onetime" label (Q2 FY26, Q1 FY27). Each individual "onetime" label is defensible on its own terms (a specific portfolio company's funding round is genuinely episodic), but in aggregate the "Other income" investment-gains category has appeared, at material size, in every one of the last five fiscal years (₹1,038mn FY22 → ₹1,746mn FY23 → ₹2,047mn FY24 → ₹2,669mn FY25 → ₹1,907mn FY26) per `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md` §5. A reader who takes each quarter's "onetime" language at face value could underestimate how structurally recurring this income source is as a category, even though no single label is technically false. This is a real, cited disclosure-quality nuance, not a fabricated impression — **RF-DISC-002 is triggered at Medium severity.** | `Q3 FY23 transcript ("onetime realized and unrealized gain… primarily from ProcMart")`; `Q4 FY24 transcript ("onetime net fair value gain… primarily ProcMart")`; `Q3 FY26 transcript ("onetime fair valuation gain… Baldor Technologies")`; `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md`, §5, §7 |
| GAAP-to-adjusted gap (magnitude / trend) | Not applicable in the formal sense (no company-defined adjusted metric exists to gap against GAAP). The closest analogue — CIQ's own vendor-constructed "Excl. Unusual Items" line, which strips the recurring investment gains — shows a 30.1% gap on Profit Before Tax and a 40.3% gap on diluted EPS in FY26. This is a **vendor construction**, not a company-published adjusted figure, so it is not scored as company non-GAAP aggressiveness, but it quantifies exactly how much of headline profit the "onetime"-labeled items represent in aggregate. | `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md`, §7 |
| Does adjusted framing flatter reality? | No formal adjusted framing exists to flatter anything — EBIT and EBITDA (the metrics management most emphasizes on calls) are already computed above the "Other income" line in the Ind AS P&L structure and are clean of the investment-gains distortion. It is PBT, Net Income, and diluted EPS (secondary emphasis on calls, always disclosed alongside EBITDA, never presented as the sole headline) that carry the non-operating gain. Management does not appear to lean on the inflated PBT/EPS figure to obscure a weaker EBITDA story — cash-flow and EBITDA figures are given first and given prominence in every prepared-remarks section reviewed. | `earnings/06_earnings-quality.md`, §7, §9; direct review of prepared-remarks ordering across all reviewed transcripts |

## 3. Disclosure Transparency

Management discloses segment economics with real granularity: Busy Infotech's own collection, revenue, deferred revenue, cash flow from operations, and new-license counts are broken out every quarter, and the core marketplace's paying-supplier count, net additions/declines, and even tier-specific churn rates (silver-tier monthly churn quantified at "about 7%" on the Q1 FY27 call) are disclosed even when the numbers are unfavorable. The clearest positive signal is that the raw net-add/net-decline number kept being reported every quarter after the *forward guidance* on that number was withdrawn in Apr-2025 — management stopped forecasting the metric but never stopped reporting it, which is the correct distinction between withdrawing a promise and hiding a result. No disclosure identified in the reviewed pool got *less* transparent over time; if anything, disclosure granularity increased (buyer-count trends, silver-tier cohort churn, and buyer-monetization experiments were all added to the Q&A record only in the more recent FY26–FY27 calls, as the underlying issues became more material).

## 4. Tone in Bad Times

The worst recent period by the numbers is Q1 FY27 (Jul-2026 call): revenue growth slowed to 11%, the core paying-supplier base declined by 1,850 net, and management openly admitted the buyer-side funnel is "stagnating." The tone was candid, not promotional — the CEO led prepared remarks with the negative supplier number and its two named causes before any question was asked, and the Q&A that followed answered specific, pointed analyst questions (on buyer monetization, churn cohort analysis, silver-tier renewal-rate trajectory, and whether the churn problem might be structural rather than transient) with specific figures rather than deflection — e.g., breaking down the buyer decline into "monetizable buyer" growth (positive) versus overall buyer count (declining), and directly stating "we did cut down on some of the non-quality buyers" as a deliberate trade-off rather than an unexplained decline. The same pattern holds on the Q4 FY25 call (the sharpest miss in the dataset, where net-add guidance was withdrawn): the CEO's direct answer — "I'm really not confident to give you any answer as of now" — is the kind of blunt non-answer that a promotional management avoids giving; it costs credibility in the moment to say it, which is itself evidence of candor over spin.

## 5. Candor Read

Across five verifiable misses spanning FY24–FY27, management consistently led with the bad number in prepared remarks rather than waiting to be asked, paired every miss with a specific, checkable driver (percentage breakdowns, named causes, or a blunt "I don't know"), and did not stop reporting an unfavorable KPI once it stopped forecasting it. The strongest evidence for trust is the Q4 FY25 exchange where the CEO declined to fabricate a recovery number under direct analyst pressure — a promotional management manufactures reassurance in that moment; this one did not. The strongest evidence against a clean bill is the RF-DISC-002 nuance on "onetime" investment-gain labeling (individually defensible, collectively understates a genuinely recurring income category) and the still-unfilled guidance vacuum on the core net-add metric more than a year after it was withdrawn — a real, if narrow, transparency gap. The routed integrity signal from `business-model/01_disqualifier-scan` (Designated Person pledge/closed-window trade) was independently investigated by `01_management-and-track-record.md` at the primary source (FY26 Secretarial Audit Report) and found disclosed, self-reported, and immaterial — it does not compound against the trust read here, consistent with `01`'s conclusion that RF-MGT-005 does not apply. **Verdict: candid / high-trust**, with the RF-DISC-002 nuance the one item worth continued monitoring rather than dismissing.

RF-DISC-002 (recurring "one-off" / aggressive non-GAAP add-backs)

| Specificity vs boilerplate | 7 | 10 | Explanations consistently cite specific percentages, named causes, and cohort-level detail rather than generic "headwinds" language, though the qualitative "2-3 quarters" churn-resolution timeline (repeated across multiple calls without a firmer date) is the one recurring boilerplate-adjacent phrase |
